\displaystyle\hat{S}_{k,1}=-\mathbf{P}_{0}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}\left(\partial_{t}\hat{F}_{k-2}-\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\hat{F}_{k-2}\right)\right\},

and

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=k\\
i,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}[Q(F^{0}_{i}+\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}^{0},\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j},F^{0}_{i}+\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}^{0})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{i},\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j})]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j+2l=k\\
b\geq l\geq 1,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{(-\eta)^{l}}{l!}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}[Q(\partial_{r}^{l}\mu_{0},\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j},\partial_{r}^{l}\mu_{0})]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j+2l=k\\
i,j\geq 1,b\geq l\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{(-\eta)^{l}}{l!}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}[Q(\partial_{r}^{l}\bar{\mathfrak{F}}^{0}_{i},\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j},\partial_{r}^{l}\bar{\mathfrak{F}}^{0}_{i})]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j+l=k\\
i,j\geq 1,b\geq l\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta^{l}}{l!}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}[Q(\partial_{y}^{l}\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}^{0},\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{j},\partial_{y}^{l}\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}^{0})]

(2.12)

\displaystyle\quad-(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}\left(\partial_{t}\hat{F}_{k-2}-\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\hat{F}_{k-2}\right)\right\}.

For later use, we define
F_{k}=0,\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{k}=0
and
\hat{F}_{k}=0
for
k<0.
It is easy to know that
\hat{S}_{k,1}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}
,
\hat{S}_{k,2}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}
. As
\hat{F}_{-1}=\hat{F}_{0}=0
, it also holds that

(2.13)

\hat{S}_{1}=\hat{S}_{1,1}=\hat{S}_{1,2}=\hat{S}_{2,1}=0.

Noting (
2.11
), we can rewrite
\hat{S}_{k,1}
as

(2.14)

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{k,1}=\left\{\hat{a}_{k}+\frac{\hat{b}_{k,1}}{T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}+\frac{\hat{b}_{k,2}}{T^{0}}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})+\frac{\hat{c}_{k}}{T^{0}}(|v-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})\right\}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}.

Lemma 2.9
.

Assume there exists some positive constant
\sigma_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|e^{\sigma_{1}\eta}(\hat{a}_{k},\hat{b}_{k},\hat{c}_{k})(t,\phi,\eta)\right|<\infty,

then there exists a function

(2.15)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{k,1}=\left\{\frac{\hat{A}_{k}}{T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}+\frac{\hat{B}_{k}}{T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})+\frac{\hat{C}_{k}}{T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})+\hat{D}_{k}\right\}\sqrt{\mu_{0}},

such that

(2.16)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\hat{f}_{k,1}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{f}_{k,1}\right)-\hat{S}_{k,1}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp},

and

\displaystyle\left|\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\hat{f}_{k,1}+G(\eta)(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})-G(\eta)\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{f}_{k,1}+\mathbf{L}_{0}\hat{f}_{k,1}-\hat{S}_{k,1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\eta}^{d}|(\hat{a}_{k},\hat{b}_{k},\hat{c}_{k})|(t,\phi,z)dz\cdot(1+|\mathfrak{v}|)^{4}\sqrt{\mu_{0}},|\hat{f}_{k,1}(t,\phi,\eta,\mathfrak{v})|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\eta}^{d}|(\hat{a}_{k},\hat{b}_{k},\hat{c}_{k})|dz\cdot(1+|\mathfrak{v}|)^{3}\sqrt{\mu_{0}},

where
(\hat{A}_{k},\hat{B}_{k},\hat{C}_{k},\hat{D}_{k})(t,\phi,r)
solves a ODEs and has explicit expression decided by
(\hat{a}_{k},\hat{b}_{k},\hat{c}_{k})
. See Appendix 6.2 for details.

Remark 2.10
.

Based on (
2.11
), we observe that
\hat{S}_{k,1}
is exclusively determined by
\hat{f}_{k-2}.
This implies that
\hat{f}_{k,1}
only depends on
\hat{f}_{k-2}
, which is known in our existence analysis for
\hat{f}_{k}
. We now investigate the equation for
\hat{f}_{k,2}
:

(2.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,2}}{\partial\eta}+G(\eta)(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,2}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,2}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{f}_{k,2}+\mathbf{L}_{0}\hat{f}_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\hat{S}_{k,2}-\mathbf{L}_{0}\hat{f}_{k,1}-\left\{\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\eta}+G(\eta)(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{k,1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{f}_{k,2}-\hat{S}_{k,1}\right\}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}.

With suitable boundary condition, one solves
\hat{f}_{k,2}
by applying Theorem
1.1
. Then

(2.18)

\hat{f}_{k}=\hat{f}_{k,1}+\hat{f}_{k,2}

is the solution to Knudsen layer problem (
1.32
).

2.4.
Boundary conditions

To construct the solutions for interior expansion, viscous and Knudsen boundary layers, the remain problem is to determine suitable boundary conditions for well-posedness. As mentioned in Remark
2.4
, we require that each
F_{k}+\bar{F}_{k}+\hat{F}_{k}
satisfies the specular reflection boundary condition, i.e.,

(2.19)

(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}+\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{k}+\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k})|_{\gamma_{-}}=(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}+\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{k}+\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k})(t,x,R_{x}v),

which, together with (
2.18
), yields

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{k,2}(t,0,\phi,\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0}

\displaystyle=\hat{f}_{k,2}(t,0,\phi,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})+f_{k}(t,1,\phi,R_{x}v)+\bar{f}_{k}(t,0,\phi,R_{y}\bar{v})

\displaystyle+\hat{f}_{k,1}(t,0,\phi,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})-f_{k}(t,1,\phi,v)-\bar{f}_{k}(t,0,\phi,\bar{v})-\hat{f}_{k,1}(t,0,\phi,\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}).

Denote

(2.20)

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{k}(t,\phi,\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})=\begin{cases}0,\quad\mathfrak{v}_{1}\textgreater 0,\\
f_{k}(t,1,\phi,v)+\bar{f}_{k}(t,0,\phi,\bar{v})-f_{k}(t,1,\phi,R_{x}v)-\bar{f}_{k}(t,0,\phi,R_{y}\bar{v})\\
\ +\hat{f}_{k,1}(t,0,\phi,\mathfrak{v})-\hat{f}_{k,1}(t,0,\phi,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\quad\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0.\end{cases}

We derive the boundary condition for (
2.17
) as

(2.21)

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{k,2}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0}=\hat{f}_{k,2}(0,R\mathfrak{v})+\hat{g}_{k}(R\mathfrak{v}),\quad\hat{f}_{k,2}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}=0.

Noting from Theorem
1.1
, to solve (
2.17
) and (
2.21
), we need
\hat{g}_{k}
to satisfy (
1.37
), i.e.

(2.22)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\hat{g}_{k}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{g}_{k}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}\hat{g}_{k}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v}=0,

which yields the following lemma on boundary conditions.

Lemma 2.11

(
[
32
]
)
.

The boundary condition for (
2.3
) is

\displaystyle(u_{k}\cdot\vec{n})|_{r=1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\{\frac{1}{\rho^{0}}\partial_{t}\bar{\rho}_{k-1}-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)[\partial_{\phi}(\rho^{0}\bar{u}_{k-1}\cdot\vec{\tau})+\partial_{\phi}(\bar{\rho}_{k-1}u_{\tau}^{0})]

(2.23)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)(\bar{u}_{k-1}\cdot\vec{n})\Big\}(y)dy+\hat{A}_{k}(0),

which is decided by
f_{i},\bar{f}_{i}(i\leq k-1),\hat{f}_{j}(j\leq k-2).

The boundary condition for (
2.7
) is

(2.24)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{y}(\bar{u}_{k-1}\cdot\vec{\tau})(t,\phi,0)=\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}(T^{0})}\Big\{\left[\rho^{0}(\bar{u}_{k-1}\cdot\vec{n})(-u^{0}_{1}\cdot\vec{\tau}+\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\vec{\tau})\right]|_{y=0}+T^{0}\langle\mathcal{A}_{12}^{0},\bar{J}_{k-2}\rangle|_{y=0}\\
\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+T^{0}\langle\mathcal{A}_{12}^{0},(\mathbf{I-P}_{0})f_{k}\rangle|_{r=1}+\rho^{0}T^{0}\hat{B}_{k}(0)\Big\},\\
\displaystyle\partial_{y}\bar{\theta}_{k-1}(t,\phi,0)=\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}(T^{0})}\Big\{\rho^{0}(\bar{u}_{k-1}\cdot\vec{n})(\theta^{0}_{1}+\bar{\theta}_{1})|_{y=0}+\frac{1}{2}(2T^{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}\langle\mathcal{B}_{1}^{0},\bar{J}_{k-2}\rangle|_{y=0}\\
\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2}(2T^{0})^{\frac{3}{2}}\langle\mathcal{B}_{1}^{0},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{k}\rangle|_{r=1}+4\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}\hat{C}_{k}(0)\Big\}.\end{cases}

The RHS of (
2.24
) is decided by
f_{i}(i\leq k-1),\bar{f}_{j},\hat{f}_{l}(j,l\leq k-2)
. The notations
\hat{A}_{k}(0),\hat{B}_{k}(0),\hat{C}_{k}(0)
are defined in (
2.15
).

Proof.

A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\left\{f_{k}(R_{x}v)-f_{k}(v)\right\}|_{r=1}\,d\mathfrak{v}=-\int_{v\cdot\vec{n}>0}(v\cdot\vec{n})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\left\{f_{k}(R_{x}v)-f_{k}(v)\right\}|_{r=1}\,dv\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(v\cdot\vec{n})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}(v)|_{r=1}\,dv,\\
\int_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\left\{\bar{f}_{k}(R_{y}\bar{v})-\bar{f}_{k}(\bar{v})\right\}|_{y=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{v}\cdot\vec{n})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}(\bar{v})|_{y=0}\,d\bar{v},\\
\int_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\left\{\hat{f}_{k,1}(-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})-\hat{f}_{k}(\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})\right\}|_{\eta=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k,1}(\mathfrak{v})|_{\eta=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}.\end{split}

which,, together with (
2.22
), yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(v\cdot\vec{n})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}(v)|_{r=1}\,dv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{v}\cdot\vec{n})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{k}(\bar{v})|_{y=0}\,d\bar{v}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k,1}(\mathfrak{v})|_{\eta=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}=0.

Similarly, we have from
\eqref{2.23}_{2,3}
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(v\cdot\vec{n})(v\cdot\vec{\tau}-\mathfrak{u}^{0}\cdot\vec{\tau})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}(v)|_{r=1}\,dv+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{v}\cdot\vec{n})(\bar{v}\cdot\vec{\tau}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{k}(\bar{v})|_{y=0}\,d\bar{v}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k,1}(\mathfrak{v})|_{\eta=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}=0,\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(v\cdot\vec{n})(|v-\mathfrak{u}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f_{k}(v)|_{r=1}\,dv-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{v}\cdot\vec{n})(|\bar{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{k}(\bar{v})|_{y=0}\,d\bar{v}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|v-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k,1}(\mathfrak{v})|_{\eta=0}\,d\mathfrak{v}=0.\end{split}

Then, a delicate calculation shows (
2.23
) and (
2.24
). See arguments in (
6.12
)–(
6.18
) for details.
∎

The condition (
1.36
) in Theorem
1.1
requires

(2.25)

\displaystyle|w_{\beta+1}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\hat{g}_{k}|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}+\left|\nu^{-1}w_{\beta}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})\hat{g}_{k}(t,\phi,0,\cdot)\right|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty.

This will be proved in Proposition
5.1
.

3.
Estimate on linear hyperbolic and parabolic systems

3.1.
Estimate on linear hyperbolic system

To study the existence of interior expansion, by noting Lemma
2.3
, we consider the following linear problem for
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},\tilde{\theta})(t,x)
:

(3.1)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}+\text{div}(\rho\tilde{u}+\tilde{\rho}\mathfrak{u})=0,\\
\displaystyle\rho\{\partial_{t}\tilde{u}+(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\mathfrak{u}+(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla)\tilde{u}\}-\frac{\tilde{\rho}}{\rho}\nabla(\rho T)+\nabla(\frac{\rho\tilde{\theta}+2\tilde{\rho}T}{2})=g_{1},\\
\displaystyle\rho\{\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}+(\tilde{\theta}\text{div}\mathfrak{u}+2T\text{div}\tilde{u})+\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}+2\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla T\}=g_{2},\end{cases}

where
(t,x)\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]\times B_{1}
. For (
3.1
), we impose

(3.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}\cdot\vec{n}(t,x)=d_{0}(t,x),\quad|x|=1,

and

(3.3)

\displaystyle(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},\tilde{\theta})(0,x)=(\tilde{\rho}_{0},\tilde{u}_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0})(x).

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
(\rho,\mathfrak{u},T)
is the smooth solution of compressible Euler equations obtained in Lemma
2.1
. We assume that

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{\rho}_{0},\tilde{u}_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0})\|_{H^{k}(B_{1})}^{2}+\sup_{t\in(0,\tau^{\delta}]}\{\|(g_{1},g_{2})(t)\|_{H^{k+1}(B_{1})}^{2}+\|d_{0}(t)\|_{H^{k+2}(B_{1})}^{2}\}\textless\infty,

with
k\geq 3
. If (
3.2
)–(
3.3
) satisfy the compatibility condition(the boundary condition (
3.2
) and the time-derivatives of initial data
(\tilde{\rho}_{0},\tilde{u}_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0})
are defined through system (
3.1
) inductively.) Then there exists a unique smooth solution
(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},\tilde{\theta})
of (
3.1
)–(
3.3
) satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]}\|(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{u},\tilde{\theta})\|_{H^{k}(B_{1})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(\tau^{\delta},E_{k+2})\left\{\|(\tilde{\rho}_{0},\tilde{u}_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0})\|_{H^{k}(B_{1})}^{2}\right.

(3.4)

\displaystyle\left.+\sup_{t\in(0,\tau^{\delta}]}\left[\|(g_{1},g_{2})(t)\|_{H^{k+1}(B_{1})}^{2}+\|d_{0}(t)\|_{H^{k+2}}^{2}\right]\right\}.

where

E_{k}:=\sup_{t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]}\|(\rho-1,\mathfrak{u},T-1)(t)\|_{H^{k}}=\sum_{0\leq i+j+l\leq k}\|(\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{x_{1}}^{j}\partial_{x_{2}}^{l})(\rho-1,\mathfrak{u},T-1)\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1: Reformulation.
Define
\tilde{p}=\frac{\rho\tilde{\theta}+2\tilde{\rho}T}{2}
. To deal with the boundary terms, it is more convenient to use the variables
(\tilde{p},\tilde{u},\tilde{\theta})
. Then (
3.1
) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{p}+\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{p}+2p\,\text{div}\tilde{u}+2\tilde{p}\,\text{div}\mathfrak{u}+\nabla{p}\cdot\tilde{u}=\frac{1}{2}g_{2},\\
\displaystyle\rho\partial_{t}\tilde{u}+\rho\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{u}+\nabla\tilde{p}-\frac{\nabla p}{p}\tilde{p}+\rho\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla\mathfrak{u}+\frac{\nabla p}{2T}\tilde{\theta}=g_{1},\\
\displaystyle\rho\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}+\rho\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}+2p\,\text{div}\tilde{u}+2\rho\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla T+\rho\tilde{\theta}\,\text{div}\mathfrak{u}=g_{2}.\end{cases}

Let
\chi(s)
be a monotone smooth cut-off function such that

(3.5)

\displaystyle\chi(s)=\begin{cases}1,\quad s\in[\frac{3}{4},1],\\
0,\quad s\in[0,\frac{1}{2}].\end{cases}

Define

\displaystyle u_{d}(t,x)\cdot\vec{n}=d_{0}(t,x)\chi(|x|),\quad u_{d}(t,x)\cdot\vec{\tau}=0,

and
\tilde{w}=\tilde{u}-u_{d}.
Then (
3.1
) can be rewritten as

(3.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\tilde{p}+\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{p}+2p\,\text{div}\tilde{w}+2\tilde{p}\,\text{div}\mathfrak{u}+\nabla{p}\cdot\tilde{w}=G_{0},\\
\rho\partial_{t}\tilde{w}+\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla)\tilde{w}+\nabla\tilde{p}-\frac{\nabla p}{p}\tilde{p}+\rho(\tilde{w}\cdot\nabla)\mathfrak{u}+\frac{\nabla p}{2T}\tilde{\theta}=G_{1},\\
\rho\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}+\rho\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}+2p\,\text{div}\tilde{w}+2\rho\tilde{w}\cdot\nabla T+\rho\tilde{\theta}\,\text{div}\mathfrak{u}=G_{2},\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle G_{0}=\frac{1}{2}g_{2}-2p\,\text{div}u_{d}-\nabla p\cdot u_{d},

\displaystyle G_{1}=g_{1}-\rho\partial_{t}u_{d}-\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\nabla)u_{d}-\rho(u_{d}\cdot\nabla)\mathfrak{u},

\displaystyle G_{2}=g_{2}-2p\,\text{div}u_{d}-2\rho\,u_{d}\cdot\nabla T.

The boundary condition of (
3.6
) becomes

(3.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{w}\cdot\vec{n}=0,\quad|x|=1.

Now we display (
3.6
) with symmetric structure:

(3.8)

\displaystyle A_{0}\partial_{t}U+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}A_{i}\partial_{i}U+A_{3}U=\mathcal{G},

where

\displaystyle U=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{p}\\
\tilde{w}\\
\tilde{\theta}\end{pmatrix}\quad,\quad A_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{3}{2}&0&0&-\rho\\
0&\rho p&0&0\\
0&0&\rho p&0\\
-\rho&0&0&\rho^{2}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle A_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{u}_{1}&p&0&-\rho\mathfrak{u}_{1}\\
p&\rho p\mathfrak{u}_{1}&0&0\\
0&0&\rho p\mathfrak{u}_{1}&0\\
-\rho\mathfrak{u}_{1}&0&0&\rho^{2}\mathfrak{u}_{1}\end{pmatrix}\quad,\quad A_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{u}_{2}&0&p&-\rho\mathfrak{u}_{2}\\
0&\rho p\mathfrak{u}_{2}&0&0\\
p&0&\rho p\mathfrak{u}_{2}&0\\
-\rho\mathfrak{u}_{2}&0&0&\rho^{2}\mathfrak{u}_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

The matrix
A_{3}
and column vector
\mathcal{G}
can be easily write down, and we do not give the details here. It is easy to check that
A_{0}
is positive.

Since
A_{n}:=\sum_{i=1}^{2}A_{i}x_{i},
is singular at the boundary
\partial B_{1}
due to
\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n}|_{\partial B_{1}}=0
, hence the problem (
3.8
) with (
3.7
) is a linear hyperbolic system with characteristic boundary. We refer to
[
10
,
52
,
53
]
for the local existence of smooth solutions.

It follows from Newtonian-Leibniz formula that

\displaystyle\|U(t)\|_{H^{k-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{0}\|_{H^{k-1}}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}U(s)\|_{H^{k-1}}\|U(s)\|_{H^{k-1}}ds

(3.9)

\displaystyle\leq\|U_{0}\|_{H^{k-1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|U(s)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}ds.

Hence we need only to close the highest order derivatives estimates.

Step 2: Internal estimate.

Let
l+|\alpha|=k
and applying
\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
to (
3.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle A_{0}\partial_{t}\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}U+\sum_{i=1}^{2}A_{i}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}U

(3.10)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathcal{G}-\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(A_{3}U)-[\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha},A_{0}]\partial_{t}U-\sum_{i=1}^{2}[\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha},A_{i}]\partial_{i}U,

where and whereafter the notation
[\cdot,\,\cdot]
denote the commutator operator, i.e.,

\displaystyle[\partial^{\alpha},f]g=\partial^{\alpha}(fg)-f\partial^{\alpha}g=\sum_{|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{2}|=\alpha,|\alpha_{1}|\geq 1}C_{\upsilon,\gamma}\partial^{\alpha_{1}}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha_{2}}g.

Multiplying (
3.10
) by
(\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(U^{\top}))\cdot(1-\chi(|x|))^{2}
and integrating the resultant equation over
[0,t]\times B_{1}
, we obtain

(3.11)

\displaystyle\|((1-\chi)\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}U)(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}U(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(E_{k+1})\int_{0}^{t}\|(U,\mathcal{G})(s)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}ds,

where the boundary term vanishes since
1-\chi=0
on the boundary.

Step 3: Estimate near the boundary.

Taking polar coordinate for (
3.8
), then it is rewritten as

(3.12)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{0}\partial_{t}\tilde{U}+\tilde{A}_{1}\partial_{r}\tilde{U}+\tilde{A}_{2}\partial_{\phi}\tilde{U}+\tilde{A}_{3}\tilde{U}=\tilde{\mathcal{G}}.

Here
\tilde{A}_{0}=A_{0},\tilde{\mathcal{G}}=(G_{0},G_{1}\cdot\vec{n},G_{1}\cdot\vec{\tau},G_{2})^{\top}
and
\tilde{U}=(\tilde{p},\tilde{w}\cdot\vec{n},\tilde{w}\cdot\vec{\tau},\tilde{\theta})^{\top}
. By direct calculation, one obtains

(3.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{3}{2}(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})&p&0&-\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})\\
p&\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})&0&0\\
0&0&\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})&0\\
-\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})&0&0&\rho^{2}(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{n})\end{pmatrix},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{3}{2r}(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0&\frac{p}{r}&-\frac{1}{r}\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})\\
0&\frac{1}{r}\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0&0\\
\frac{p}{r}&0&\frac{1}{r}\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0\\
-\frac{1}{r}\rho(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0&0&\frac{1}{r}\rho^{2}(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})\end{pmatrix}.

We notice that

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{3}=A_{3}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{p}{r}&0&0\\
0&0&-\frac{1}{r}\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{r}\rho p(\mathfrak{u}\cdot\vec{\tau})&0&0\\
0&0&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

The expression of
\tilde{A}_{2}
and
\tilde{A}_{3}
has singularity of
r^{-1}
. We deal with it by using the cut-off function
\chi(r)
which is
0
when
r\leq\frac{1}{2}
. We also notice that when
r>\frac{1}{2}
, one has

(3.14)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial(x_{1},x_{2})}{\partial(r,\phi)}\right|=r\sim 1.

The procedure to get energy estimate for (
3.12
) is almost the same as half space problem in
[
32
]
and we omit it here for brevity. We obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|=k-l}\|\chi(\partial_{t,\phi}^{\alpha}\partial_{r}^{l}\tilde{U})(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{k+1})\int_{0}^{t}\|U(s)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\|\mathcal{G}(s)\|_{H^{k+1}}^{2}ds

(3.15)

\displaystyle\quad+C(E_{k+1})\|(U,\mathcal{G})(0)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}.

Hence we conclude from (
3.11
) and (
3.15
) that (
3.4
) is true and the proof of Lemma
3.1
is finished.
∎

3.2.
Estimate on linear parabolic system

To construct viscous boundary layer solutions, noting Lemma
2.6
, we consider linear parabolic system for
(u_{\tau},\theta)
:

(3.16)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho^{0}\partial_{t}u_{\tau}-\kappa_{1}(T^{0})\partial_{yy}u_{\tau}\\
\quad-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\rho^{0}u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}u_{\tau}+\rho^{0}(-y\partial_{r}\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}\cdot\vec{n})\partial_{y}u_{\tau}\\
\quad+u_{\tau}\cdot\left\{\left(1-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}\rho^{0}+\left(1-2\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)\rho^{0}\partial_{\phi}u_{\tau}^{0}\right\}\\
\quad-\frac{\rho^{0}}{2T^{0}}\theta\cdot\left\{\left(1-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}u_{\tau}^{0}+\partial_{\phi}T^{0}+\frac{T^{0}}{\rho^{0}}\partial_{\phi}\rho^{0}\right\}=\bar{g}_{1},\\
\\
\rho^{0}\partial_{t}\theta-\kappa_{2}(T^{0})\partial_{yy}\theta-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\rho^{0}u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}\theta+\rho^{0}(-y\partial_{r}\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}\cdot\vec{n})\partial_{y}\theta\\
\quad+\theta\cdot\left\{\left(1-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)\partial_{\phi}(\rho^{0}u_{\tau}^{0})-\frac{\rho^{0}}{T^{0}}\left(1-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}T^{0}-\rho^{0}\partial_{\phi}u_{\tau}^{0}-\rho^{0}\partial_{r}\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}\cdot\vec{n}\right\}\\
\quad+\rho^{0}u_{\tau}\cdot\left\{\left(1-2\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)\partial_{\phi}T^{0}+\frac{T^{0}}{\rho^{0}}\partial_{\phi}\rho^{0}+\left(1-\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)\right)u_{\tau}^{0}\partial_{\phi}u_{\tau}^{0}\right\}=\bar{g}_{2},\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{H}^{\varepsilon}(y)=\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon y}\Upsilon(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}y)
and
(t,\phi,y)\in[0,\tau^{\delta})\times[0,2\pi]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
(\rho^{0},u_{\tau}^{0},\partial_{r}\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0},\partial_{r}p^{0},\partial_{\phi}p^{0})
represents values at
r=1
, and are independent of
y
. We impose non-homogeneous Neumann boundary condition for (
3.16
), i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{y}u_{\tau}(t,\phi,y)|_{y=0}=b(t,\phi),\quad\partial_{y}\theta(t,\phi,y)|_{y=0}=a(t,\phi),\\
\lim\limits_{y\rightarrow\infty}(u_{\tau},\theta)(t,\phi,y)=0,\end{cases}

We also impose the initial data

(3.17)

\displaystyle u_{\tau}(t,\phi,y)|_{t=0}=u_{0}(\phi,y),\quad\theta(t,\phi,y)|_{t=0}=\theta_{0}(\phi,y).

The initial data
(u_{0},\theta_{0})
should satisfy the compatible condition.

Let
l\geq 0
, denote

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l}}=\iint(1+y)^{l}|f(\phi,y)|^{2}d\phi dy

\displaystyle\bar{x}:=(\phi,y),\quad\nabla_{\bar{x}}=(\partial_{\phi},\partial_{y}),

we have the following lemma. For the proof of Lemma
3.2
, we refer the readers to
[
32
]
, which establishes existence and regularity for parabolic systems in 3D half-space. While the original work focuses on three dimensions, the framework extends to the 2D case (
3.16
) with minor geometric adjustments. These modifications preserve the core analytical structure of the proof; full details are omitted here for brevity.

Lemma 3.2

(
[
32
]
)
.

Assume
l\geq 0,k\geq 2
, and the compatibility condition for the initial data (
3.16
) is satisfied. Assume

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]}\left\{\sum\limits_{\alpha+2i\leq k+2}\|\partial_{\phi}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{i}(a,b)(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+2i=j}\|\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{i}(\bar{g}_{1},\bar{g}_{2})(t)\|_{L_{l_{j}}^{2}}^{2}\right\}<\infty,

where
l_{j}:=l+2(k-j),0\leq j\leq k.
Then there is a unique smooth solution
(u_{\tau},\theta)
of (
3.16
)–(
3.17
) which satisfies

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+2i=j}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]}\left\{\|\partial_{t}^{i}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}(u_{\tau},\theta)(t)\|_{L^{2}_{l_{j}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{i}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}(u_{\tau},\theta)\|_{L_{l_{j}}^{2}}^{2}ds\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tau,E_{k+3})\left\{\sum\limits_{j=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+2i=j}\|\partial_{t}^{i}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}(u_{\tau},\theta)(0)\|_{L^{2}_{l_{j}}}^{2}\right\}

(3.18)

\displaystyle+\sup_{t\in(0,\tau^{\delta}]}\left\{\sum\limits_{\alpha+2i\leq k+2}\|\partial_{\phi}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{i}(a,b)(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{k}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+2i=j}\|\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{i}(\bar{g}_{1},\bar{g}_{2})(t)\|_{L_{l_{j}}^{2}}^{2}\right\},

where the notation
E_{k+3}
is defined in Lemma
3.1
.

4.
The Existence of Knudsen boundary layer problem

In this section, we recall that
\mathfrak{v}=(\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})
and
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}=(0,u_{\tau}^{0})
.
For (
1.33
), we define
f^{c}(\eta,\mathfrak{v}):=f(\eta,\mathfrak{v})+\Upsilon(\eta)f_{b}(\mathfrak{v})
, then the equation for
f^{c}
is

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f^{c}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f^{c}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f^{c}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}\cdot G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f^{c}+\mathbf{L}_{0}f^{c}=S,\\
\displaystyle f^{c}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0}=f^{c}(0,R\mathfrak{v}),\\
f^{c}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}=0,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}+\partial_{\eta}\Upsilon(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}f_{b}+G(\eta)\Upsilon(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f_{b}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f_{b}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)

(4.2)

\displaystyle-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\Upsilon(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f_{b}+\Upsilon(\eta)\mathbf{L}_{0}f_{b}.

The solvable condition (
1.37
) and
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}
in Theorem
1.1
are actually

(4.3)

\displaystyle\langle S,(1,\mathfrak{v}_{2},|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\rangle\equiv 0.

Then one considers

(4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f+G(\eta)(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f+\mathbf{L}_{0}f=S,\\
\displaystyle f(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=f(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\\
\displaystyle f(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{cases}

where
(\eta,\mathfrak{v})\in\Omega_{d}:=[0,d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
d=\varepsilon^{-\mathfrak{a}}
and
\mathfrak{a}<\frac{2}{3}
, and
G(\eta)=-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}
. The source term
S
satisfies (
4.3
). The notation
\gamma_{-}
here denotes
\{(0,\mathfrak{v})|\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0\}\cup\{(d,\mathfrak{v})|\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0\}
.

We proceed to specify how the refined
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
framework, combined with the smallness of
\varepsilon
and the constructed zero incoming condition at
\eta=d
, systematically establishes the existence of solutions to (
4.4
).

Noting the definition of
\mathbf{L}_{0}
in (
1.23
), one has that

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{0}f

\displaystyle=\nu(\mathfrak{v})f-Kf=\nu(\mathfrak{v})f-(K_{1}f-K_{2}f)

\displaystyle=\nu(\mathfrak{v})f-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{1}(\mathfrak{v},u)f(u)\,du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{2}(\mathfrak{v},u)f(u)\,du\right),

where
\nu(\mathfrak{v})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\mathfrak{v}-u|\mu_{0}(u)\,du\cong 1+|\mathfrak{v}|
and

\displaystyle K_{1}f

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{1}(\mathfrak{v},u)f(u)\,du=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\mathfrak{v}-u|\sqrt{\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})}\sqrt{\mu_{0}(u)}f(u)\,du

\displaystyle K_{2}f

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{2}(\mathfrak{v},u)f(u)\,du=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\mathfrak{v}-u|\sqrt{\mu_{0}(u)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}(v^{\prime})}f(u^{\prime})\,du.

One has from
[
23
]
that

(4.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}k_{1}(\mathfrak{v},u)\lesssim|\mathfrak{v}-u|\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{4T^{0}}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}+|u-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2})\right\},\\
k_{2}(\mathfrak{v},u)\lesssim\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{8T^{0}}\left(|\mathfrak{v}-u|^{2}+\frac{\left||\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-|u-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}\right|^{2}}{|\mathfrak{v}-u|^{2}}\right)\right\},\end{split}

and
k_{i}(\mathfrak{v},u)\in L_{u}^{1}\cap L^{2}_{u},\,i=1,2
.

For
L^{\infty}
estimate, we define
\mathbf{f}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f
, then (
4.4
) is rewritten as

(4.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\mathbf{f}+G(\eta)(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}})+\mathbf{L}_{M}\mathbf{f}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}S=:\bar{S},\\
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\mathbf{f}(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\\
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{M}\mathbf{f}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\left[Q(\mu_{0},\sqrt{\mu_{M}}\mathbf{f})+Q(\sqrt{\mu_{M}}\mathbf{f},\mu_{0})\right]=\nu\mathbf{f}-K_{M}\mathbf{f}.

From the definition of
f
and
\mathbf{f}
, one has

\displaystyle K_{M}\mathbf{f}=

\displaystyle K_{M,1}\mathbf{f}-K_{M,2}\mathbf{f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{M,1}(\mathfrak{v},u)\mathbf{f}(u)du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{M,2}(\mathfrak{v},u)\mathbf{f}(u)du,

with

\displaystyle k_{M,1}(\mathfrak{v},u)=k_{1}(\mathfrak{v},u)\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})}\sqrt{\mu_{M}(u)}}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}(u)}\sqrt{\mu_{M}(\mathfrak{v})}},\quad k_{M,2}(\mathfrak{v},u)=k_{2}(\mathfrak{v},u)\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})}\sqrt{\mu_{M}(u)}}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}(u)}\sqrt{\mu_{M}(\mathfrak{v})}}.

Lemma 4.1
.

For
k_{M}(\mathfrak{v},u)
, one has that

(4.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{M}(\mathfrak{v},u)(1+|u|^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}e^{\zeta|u|^{2}}du\leq C(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{\frac{\beta-1}{2}}e^{\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}},\quad\beta\geq 0,\zeta<\frac{1}{4T_{M}}.

Remark 4.2
.

Since we have not found a direct reference which gives Lemma
4.1
, so we present details of calculation in the Appendix 6.3.

It is known that
\mathbf{L}_{0}
is a self adjoint linear operator and for
g\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}
, there is some positive constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\langle g,\mathbf{L}_{0}g\rangle\geq c_{0}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})g\|_{\nu}^{2}.

Define
\mathbf{L}_{0}^{-1}:\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}\to\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}
as the pseudo-inverse operator of
\mathbf{L}_{0}
. In the following, we will write
w_{\beta}
as
w
for simplicity when no confusion arises.

4.1.
Existence of solution

Definition 4.1
.

We define the backward characteristic for (
4.6
) as

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{dX}{ds}=V_{1},\quad\frac{dV_{1}}{ds}=G(X(s))V_{2}^{2},\quad\frac{dV_{2}}{ds}=-G(X(s))V_{1}V_{2},\\
\displaystyle(X,V)(\mathfrak{t})=(\eta,\mathfrak{v}).\end{cases}

For late use, we define an increasing function
W(\eta)
as

\displaystyle W(\eta)=\int_{0}^{\eta}-G(z)dz=-\ln(1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta).

It is clear that

\displaystyle 0\leq W(\eta)\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\eta}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta},\quad e^{W(\eta)}-1=\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}-1=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\eta}{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}.

As in
[
59
]
, along the characteristics, it holds that

(4.9)

\displaystyle E_{1}:=V_{1}^{2}+V_{2}^{2}\equiv\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}+\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}=|\mathfrak{v}|^{2},\quad E_{2}:=V_{2}(s)e^{-W(X(s))}\equiv\mathfrak{v}_{2}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}.

For each
(\mathfrak{t},\eta,\mathfrak{v})
with
\eta\in\overline{\Omega}_{d},\mathfrak{v}_{1}\neq 0
, we define its backward time
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{b}}(\mathfrak{t},\eta,\mathfrak{v})\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{b}}(\mathfrak{t},\eta,\mathfrak{v}):=\min\left\{z\geq 0:\left(\eta-\int_{\tau}^{\mathfrak{t}}V_{1}(s)ds,V(\tau)\right)\in\gamma_{-},\ \mathfrak{t}-\tau=z\right\}.

We also define
\eta_{\mathbf{b}}(\mathfrak{t},\eta,\mathfrak{v})=\eta(\mathfrak{t})-\int_{\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{b}}}^{\mathfrak{t}}V_{1}(s)ds\in\partial\overline{\Omega}_{d}
. Let
\eta\in\overline{\Omega}_{d},\ (\eta,\mathfrak{v})\notin\{\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}\},\ (\mathfrak{t}_{0},\eta_{0},v_{0})=(\mathfrak{t},\eta,\mathfrak{v})
. We inductively define for
k\geq 0
that

(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\eta_{k+1})=(\mathfrak{t}_{k}-\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{b}}(\eta_{k},v_{k}),\eta_{\mathbf{b}}(\eta_{k},v_{k})).

The definition of
v_{k+1}
can be obtained in the following backtracking process. In fact, we divide the discussion into two cases.

Case 1.
E_{2}>|V_{2}(d)|^{2}=\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\cdot e^{2W(d)-2W(\eta)}
.
It would be discussed for
\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0
and
\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0
, respectively.

Case 1.1.
E_{1}>\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\cdot e^{2W(d)-2W(\eta)},\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0.
In this type, the backward characteristic line is traced back from
(\eta,\mathfrak{v})
to
X(s)=d
. Then one has

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle X_{cl}(s)=\eta-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}}V_{cl,1}(z)dz,\\
V_{cl}=\left(-\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right).\end{cases}

Case 1.2.
E_{1}>\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\cdot e^{2W(d)-2W(\eta)},\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0.
At this time, the backward characteristic line is traced back to
X(s)=0
first, and then traced back to
X(s)=d
due to the specular reflection condition. Then we obtain from (
4.8
)–(
4.9
) that

\displaystyle X_{cl}(s)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}]}(s)\left\{\eta-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}}V_{cl,1}(z)dz\right\}+\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{2},\mathfrak{t}_{1}]}(s)\left\{-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}V_{cl,1}(z)dz\right\},

\displaystyle V_{cl}(s)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}]}(s)\left(\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{2},\mathfrak{t}_{1}]}(s)\left(-\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right),

and the backward velocity
v_{1}=(-\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}},E_{2})
representing the velocity after collision with
\eta_{1}=0
. See the figures below for
Case 1
.

for some

, which is equivalent to

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}+\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}=\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\cdot e^{2W(\eta_{+})-2W(\eta)}.

Thus, the characteristic line cannot reach
\gamma_{-}
on

, then it will cycle between

and
\eta_{+}
. We have
\eta_{k}=0
for all

, then define

.

\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0
, we define the backward cycle as

\left\{\begin{aligned} X_{cl}(s;t,\eta,\mathfrak{v})&=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k}]}(s)\left\{\eta_{k}-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}V_{cl,1}(z)dz\right\},\\

&\quad+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k,0},\mathfrak{t}_{k}]}(s)\left(-\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k,0}]}(s)\left(\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

The notation
\mathfrak{t}_{k,0}
means that on the
k
-th cycle,
V_{1}(s)=0
at
s=\mathfrak{t}_{k,0}\in[\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k}]
.

Case 2.2.
For
\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0
, the backward cycle is

\left\{\begin{aligned} X_{cl}(s;t,\eta,\mathfrak{v})&=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k}]}(s)\left\{\eta_{k}-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}V_{cl,1}(z)dz\right\},\\
V_{cl}(s;t,\eta,\mathfrak{v})&=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k,0},\mathfrak{t}_{k}]}(s)\left(-\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k,0}]}(s)\left(\sqrt{E_{1}-E_{2}^{2}e^{2W(X(s))}},E_{2}e^{W(X(s))}\right).\end{aligned}\right.

See the following figure for

:

For later use, we define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}f_{\lambda}:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f_{\lambda}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu f_{\lambda}-\lambda Kf_{\lambda}-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f_{\lambda}.

In this subsection, we consider

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}f=S,\\
f(0,\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0}=f(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\\
f(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}=0.\end{cases}

We denote the solution of (
4.11
) as
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}S.

We start with the existence and uniform estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}S
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\beta>3
and
\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
. For

(4.12)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}f_{0}=\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f_{0}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f_{0}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f_{0}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu f_{0}-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f_{0}=S,\\
\displaystyle f_{0}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=f_{0}(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\quad f_{0}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{cases}

there is a unique solution
f_{0}
of (
4.12
) satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}}wf_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\left|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}}wf_{0}\right|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}<C\left\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
d
. Moreover, if
S
is continuous in
[0,d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, then
f_{0}
is continuous away from grazing set
\{(\eta,\mathfrak{v}):\eta=0,\mathfrak{v}_{1}=0\}
.

Proof.

Since the proof is very long, we divide it into several steps.

Step 1.
Consider the iterative scheme for (
4.12
) with
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f^{i+1}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f^{i+1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f^{i+1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu f^{i+1}-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f^{i+1}=S,\\
\displaystyle f^{i+1}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)f^{i}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\quad f^{i+1}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.\end{cases}

where
f^{0}\equiv 0
and
i=0,1,2,...
Let
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}^{i}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f^{i}
, the equation for
\mathbf{f}^{i+1}
is

(4.13)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\mathbf{f}^{i+1}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}^{i+1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}^{i+1}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu\mathbf{f}^{i+1}=\bar{S},\\
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}^{i+1}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)\mathbf{f}^{i}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\quad\mathbf{f}^{i+1}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.\end{cases}

Let
h^{i}=w\mathbf{f}^{i}
. Along the characteristic (
4.8
), we have from
\eqref{5.16}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\left\{h^{i+1}e^{\nu s}\right\}=w\bar{S}e^{\nu s}.

For
Case 1.1
, one has

(4.14)

\displaystyle h^{i+1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

In
Case 1.2
, one obtains

(4.15)

\displaystyle h^{i+1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=\sum_{l=0}^{1}(1-\frac{1}{n})^{l}\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l+1}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

Hence, for
Case 1
, we have from (
4.14
)–(
4.15
) that

(4.16)

\displaystyle h^{i+1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})\equiv h^{i}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})\equiv...\equiv h^{1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v}).

For
Case 2
, one has

\displaystyle h^{i+1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})e^{\nu t}

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{1}{n})h^{i}(\eta_{1},v_{1})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}_{1}}+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{\nu s}\,ds

\displaystyle=...

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{1}{n})^{k}h^{i+1-k}(\eta_{k},v_{k})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}_{k}}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}(1-\frac{1}{n})^{l-1}\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{\nu s}\,ds,

which yields that

\displaystyle h^{i+1}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{k}h^{i+1-k}\left(0,v_{k}\right)e^{-\nu\left(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k}\right)}

(4.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{l-1}\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

Choosing
k=i+1
, then the first term on the RHS of (
4.17
) is zero, thus we have

(4.18)

\displaystyle|h^{i+1}|\leq\left|\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}w\bar{S}\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\,ds\right|\leq\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Combining (
4.14
), (
4.15
) and (
4.18
), we obtain

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left\|h^{i+1}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}+\left|h^{i+1}\right|_{L^{\infty}\left(\gamma_{+}\right)}\leq C\left\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}},\quad i=0,1,2,\dots,

where
C>0
does not depend on
i,n
and
\varepsilon
.

Step 2.
Now we consider
h^{i+1}-h^{i}
. In
Case 1
, we have from (
4.16
) that

h^{i+1}-h^{i}\equiv 0.

For
Case 2
, taking
k=i
, one has from (
4.17
) that

\displaystyle(h^{i+1}-h^{i})(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{i}h^{1}(0,v_{i})e^{-\nu\left(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{i}\right)},

which, together with (
4.19
), yields that

\displaystyle\|h^{i+1}-h^{i}\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}+|h^{i+1}-h^{i}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{i}\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}.

It follows that
\left\{h^{i}\right\}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{\infty}
, hence there exists a
\mathbf{f}^{n}\in L^{\infty}
such that
h^{i}\to w\mathbf{f}^{n}
as
i\to\infty
. It is clear that
\mathbf{f}^{n}
solves

(4.20)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\mathbf{f}^{n}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}^{n}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}^{n}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu\mathbf{f}^{n}=\bar{S},\\
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}^{n}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)\mathbf{f}^{n}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\quad\mathbf{f}^{n}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{cases}

which is

(4.21)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}_{0}f^{n}=S,\\
f^{n}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)f^{n}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\quad f^{n}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0,\end{cases}

where
f^{n}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}\mathbf{f}^{n}
. It follows from (
4.19
) that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wf^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\left|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wf^{n}\right|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
n,d
and
\varepsilon
.

Step 3.
Now we consider the limit
n\to\infty
. Without loss of generality, we take
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
. The equation of
(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}})
is

(4.23)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}})+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)=0,\\
\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=\left(1-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\right)\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}-\frac{1}{n_{1}}\right)\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\\
\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}\right)(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.\end{cases}

To estimate (
4.23
), we first consider the following problem.

(4.24)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}g+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu g=0,\\
\displaystyle g(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=(1-\frac{1}{n})g(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})+r(-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\\
\displaystyle g(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.\end{cases}

Integrating along the backward characteristic, we have

\displaystyle g(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=\begin{cases}0,\quad\text{for}\,\text{\it Case 1.1},\\
r(v_{1})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{1})},\quad\text{for}\,\text{\it Case 1.2}.\end{cases}

For
Case 2
, one obtains

(4.25)

\displaystyle g(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=(1-\frac{1}{n})^{k}g(0,v_{k})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}(1-\frac{1}{n})^{l-1}r(v_{l})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{l})},

where the second term on the RHS brings the main difficulty since there is not a uniform bounded
k
to bound the first term for general
(\eta,\mathfrak{v})
.

In
Case 2
, it is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l+1}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}|V_{1}|\,ds=2\eta_{+}.

It follows from (
4.10
) that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\eta_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\{1-\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|}{|\mathfrak{v}|}\right\}+\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|}{|\mathfrak{v}|}\eta=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\{\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|^{2}}{|\mathfrak{v}|\cdot(|\mathfrak{v}|+|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|)}\right\}+\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|}{|\mathfrak{v}|}\eta.

If
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}>0
, one has from (
4.26
) that
(1+|\mathfrak{v}|)^{2}\eta_{+}\geq\frac{C\tilde{m}^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}>0
, so we choose
k=(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})k_{0}(\tilde{m})
with
k_{0}(\tilde{m})>0
suitably large such that

\displaystyle e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}\leq e^{-(k-1)\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}|V_{1}|\,d\tau}=e^{-2(k-1)\eta_{+}}\leq\frac{1}{8}.

It follows from (
4.9
) that
V_{2}(X(s))
increases with
X(s)
, then one has
|v_{k+1,1}|\geq\tilde{m}
for
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}
. Combining
Case 1
and
Case 2
, one obtains

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\}}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\}}g|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}|\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\}}g|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}+C_{\tilde{m}}|w_{2}r|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\leq C_{\tilde{m}}|w_{2}r|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}.

For
\eta\geq\tilde{m}^{\prime}
, one has from (
4.26
) that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\eta_{+}=\eta+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}-\eta\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|}{|\mathfrak{v}|}\right)\geq\tilde{m}^{\prime}.

So we choose
k=k_{0}^{\prime}(\tilde{m}^{\prime})
with
k_{0}^{\prime}(\tilde{m}^{\prime})>0
suitably large such that

\displaystyle e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}\leq e^{-(k-1)\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}|V_{1}|\,d\tau}=e^{-2(k-1)\eta_{+}}\leq\frac{1}{8}.

In
Case 2
, we have from (
4.25
) that

\displaystyle g(\eta,\mathfrak{v})

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{1}{n})^{k}g(0,v_{k})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}(1-\frac{1}{n})^{l-1}r(v_{l})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{l})}

(4.29)

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{1}{n})^{k}g(\eta,V_{cl}(\mathfrak{t}_{c}))e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})-\nu(\mathfrak{t}_{k}-\mathfrak{t}_{c})}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}(1-\frac{1}{n})^{l-1}r(v_{l})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{l})},

where
\mathfrak{t}_{c}\in(\mathfrak{t}_{k+1},\mathfrak{t}_{k})
is the time that the backward characteristic moves from
X(\mathfrak{t}_{k})=0
to
X(\mathfrak{t}_{c})=\eta
without reaching
\eta_{+}
. Combining
Case 1
and
Case 2
, one obtains from (
4.29
) that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\{\eta\geq\tilde{m}^{\prime}\}}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\mathbf{1}_{\{\eta\geq\tilde{m}^{\prime}\}}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C_{\tilde{m}^{\prime}}|r|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\leq C_{\tilde{m}^{\prime}}|r|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}.

Applying (
4.27
), (
4.30
) to (
4.23
), and using (
4.22
), we have

(4.31)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\}}w_{\beta-2}(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\}}w_{\beta-2}(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}})|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{m}}(\frac{1}{n_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}})|w_{\beta}\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C_{\tilde{m}}(\frac{1}{n_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}})\|\nu^{-1}w_{\beta}\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\,\to 0,\,n_{1},n_{2}\to\infty,

and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{\{\eta\geq\tilde{m}^{\prime}\}}w_{\beta}(\mathbf{f}^{n_{1}}-\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\leq C_{\tilde{m}^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{n_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}})|w_{\beta}\mathbf{f}^{n_{2}}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{m}^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{n_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}})\|\nu^{-1}w_{\beta}\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\,\to 0,\,n_{1},n_{2}\to\infty.

It follows from (
4.27
) and (
4.32
) that
w_{\beta}\mathbf{f}^{n}\to w_{\beta}\mathbf{f}_{0}
in
L^{\infty}(\tilde{m}^{\prime},d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
w_{\beta-2}\mathbf{f}^{n}\to w_{\beta-2}\mathbf{f}_{0}
in
L^{\infty}([0,d]\times\{\mathfrak{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq\tilde{m}\})
. Since
\tilde{m},\tilde{m}^{\prime}
can be arbitrary small, it is clear to know that
w_{\beta-2}\mathbf{f}^{n}\to w_{\beta-2}\mathbf{f}_{0}
a.e. in
\Omega_{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and also
f^{n}-f_{0}\to 0
in
L^{2}(\Omega_{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
f_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}\mathbf{f}_{0}
. Noting
\tilde{m}
and
\tilde{m}^{\prime}
can be any positive constants, one has from (
4.31
)–(
4.32
) that
\{\mathbf{f}^{n}\}
converges almost everywhere on
\Omega_{d}\backslash\{\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}\}
. Hence there exists a limit function
\mathbf{f}_{0}\in L^{\infty}_{w_{\beta}}
and
\displaystyle f_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}\mathbf{f}_{0}
solves (
4.12
). It follows immediately that
f_{0}\in C([0,d]\times\{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|=0\}\})\cup((0,d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
, which shows that
f_{0}
is continuous away from
\{(\eta,\mathfrak{v}):\eta=0,\mathfrak{v}_{1}=0\}
. Then Lemma
4.3
is true and the proof is finished.
∎

Now we give the
a priori
estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}S
. To establish the
L^{2}
estimate of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}S
,
we highlight a critical analytical challenge arising from the geometric curvature term
\displaystyle-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f_{\lambda}
, where the velocity product
\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}
resists standard energy methods, see
[
15
]
. In fact, this term originates from the geometric effect of the disk. By utilizing the smallness associated with
\varepsilon
in
G(\eta)
, we use
L^{\infty}
–estimate to overcome the difficulty.

Lemma 4.4
.

Assume
\beta>3
. For any given
\lambda\in[0,1]
, let
f_{\lambda}
be the solution of (
4.11
) with
\displaystyle\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
. Let
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f_{\lambda}
, then it holds that

(4.33)

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{\min\{2-3\mathfrak{a},1-\mathfrak{a}\}}\left\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}^{2}+C\max\{\varepsilon^{-2},d^{4}\}\|S\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{2}}^{2},

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\lambda
.

Proof.

The equation (
4.11
) can be rewritten as

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f_{\lambda}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+(1-\lambda)\nu f_{\lambda}+\lambda\mathbf{L}_{0}f_{\lambda}-\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f_{\lambda}=S,\\
\displaystyle f_{\lambda}(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=f_{\lambda}\left(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}\right),\quad f_{\lambda}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0.\end{cases}

Multiplying (
4.34
) by
f_{\lambda}
and integrating on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, noting the non-negativity of
\mathbf{L}_{0}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\eta}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|f_{\lambda}|^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}\right\}+(1-\lambda)\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}+\lambda c_{0}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq C\left|G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}|f_{\lambda}|^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\right|+C\left|e^{-W(\eta)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S\cdot f_{\lambda}\,d\mathfrak{v}\right|.

A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\left|G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}|f_{\lambda}|^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\right|\,d\eta\leq\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{d}|G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}|\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Then, integrating (
4.35
) over
[0,d]
and observing that
e^{-W(\eta)}\sim 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+(1-\lambda)\int_{0}^{d}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\,d\eta+\lambda c_{0}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f_{\lambda}\cdot S|\,d\mathfrak{v}\,d\eta.

The rest proof are divided into several steps.

Step 1.
For
1-\lambda\geq\varepsilon
, we have from (
4.36
) that

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{2}}^{2}+|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\|S\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{1-\mathfrak{a}}\left\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}^{2}.

Step 2.
For
1-\lambda\leq\varepsilon
,
denote

\mathbf{P}_{0}f_{\lambda}=\left\{a_{\lambda}+b_{\lambda}\cdot(\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0})+c_{\lambda}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})\right\}\sqrt{\mu_{0}},

then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}(\mathbf{P}_{0}f_{\lambda})=

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\eta}a_{\lambda}\cdot\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}+\partial_{\eta}b_{1,\lambda}\cdot\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}+\partial_{\eta}b_{2,\lambda}\cdot\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}

\displaystyle+\partial_{\eta}c_{\lambda}\cdot\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}.

Step 2.1 Estimate on
b_{2,\lambda}
.
Taking
\psi_{b,2}=\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
, it is clear that

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f_{\lambda},\psi_{b,2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathbf{P}_{0}f_{\lambda},\psi_{b,2}\rangle+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda},\psi_{b,2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}b_{2,\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{b,2},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle,

where we have used

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})^{2}\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})\,d\mathfrak{v}=\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}.

Multiplying (
4.34
) by
\psi_{b,2}
and integrating on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has that

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}\left\{\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}b_{2,\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{b,2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=G(\eta)\left\langle\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\psi_{b,2}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial(\mathfrak{v}_{2}\psi_{b,2})}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}},f_{\lambda}\right\rangle-(1-\lambda)\langle\psi_{b,2},\nu f_{\lambda}\rangle-\lambda\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f_{\lambda},\psi_{b,2}\rangle

(4.38)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\psi_{b,2},f_{\lambda}\rangle+\langle\psi_{b,2},S\rangle=:\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{I}_{b,i}.

It is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\eta}^{d}\partial_{\eta}\left\{\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}b_{2,\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{b,2},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle\right\}\,dz

(4.39)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}},f_{\lambda}(d)\rangle-[\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}b_{2,\lambda}(\eta)+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}(\eta)\rangle],

and

\displaystyle|\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}},f_{\lambda}(d)\rangle|\leq C|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}(\mathcal{I}_{b,1}+\mathcal{I}_{b,4})\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq\left(\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},\\
\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{b,2}\,dz\right|\leq(1-\lambda)\int_{\eta}^{d}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq(1-\lambda)d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},\\
\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{b,3}\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\{\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

and

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{2}^{5}\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Thus, by integrating (
4.38
) over
[\eta,d]
, it follows from (
4.39
)–(
4.40
) that

\displaystyle|b_{2,\lambda}(\eta)|

\displaystyle\leq C|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}+C\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}(\eta)\|_{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+Cd^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+Cd^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\{\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.41)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left\{(1-\lambda)d^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Subsequently, integrating (
4.41
) over
[0,d]
and noting
1-\lambda\leq\varepsilon
, one gets

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|b_{2,\lambda}|^{2}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\,Cd|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+Cd^{2}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta

(4.42)

\displaystyle\quad+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2-2\mathfrak{a}}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2},

where one has used
d=\varepsilon^{-\mathfrak{a}}
and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)dzd\eta\leq C\varepsilon^{4-2\mathfrak{a}}.

Step 2.2 Estimate on
c_{\lambda}
.
Taking
\psi_{c}=\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f_{\lambda},\psi_{c}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathbf{P}_{0}f_{\lambda},\psi_{c}\rangle+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda},\psi_{c}\rangle

\displaystyle=8\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{3}c_{\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{c},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle,

where we have used

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})d\mathfrak{v}=0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})d\mathfrak{v}=8\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{3}.

Multiplying (
4.34
) by
\psi_{c}
and integrating on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has that

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}\left\{8\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{3}c_{\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{c},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle=G(\eta)\left\langle\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\psi_{c}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial(\mathfrak{v}_{2}\psi_{c})}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}},f_{\lambda}\right\rangle-(1-\lambda)\langle\psi_{c},\nu f_{\lambda}\rangle-\lambda\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f_{\lambda},\psi_{c}\rangle

(4.43)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\psi_{c},f_{\lambda}\rangle+\langle\psi_{c},S\rangle=:\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{I}_{c,i}.

It is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\eta}^{d}\partial_{\eta}\left\{8\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{3}c_{\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{c},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle\right\}\,dz

(4.44)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{c},f_{\lambda}(d)\rangle-[8\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{3}c_{\lambda}(\eta)+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{c},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}(\eta)\rangle].

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}(\mathcal{I}_{c,1}+\mathcal{I}_{c,4})\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq\left(\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},\\
\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{c,2}\,dz\right|\leq(1-\lambda)\int_{\eta}^{d}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq(1-\lambda)d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},\\
\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{c,3}\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\{\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

and

(4.45)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\eta}^{d}\mathcal{I}_{c,5}\,dz\right|\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\leq d^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Then, by integrating (
4.43
) over
[\eta,d]
, it follows from (
4.44
)–(
4.45
) that

\displaystyle|c_{\lambda}(\eta)|

\displaystyle\leq C|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}+C\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}(\eta)\|_{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+Cd^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+Cd^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left\{\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.46)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left\{(1-\lambda)d^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Next, integrating (
4.46
) over
[0,d]
and noting
1-\lambda\leq\varepsilon
, one gets

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|c_{\lambda}|^{2}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\,Cd|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+Cd^{2}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta

(4.47)

\displaystyle\quad+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2-2\mathfrak{a}}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2.3 Estimate on
a_{\lambda}
.
Taking
\psi_{a}=-\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-8T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
, one has that

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f_{\lambda},\psi_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathbf{P}_{0}f_{\lambda},\psi_{a}\rangle+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda},\psi_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=4\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}a_{\lambda}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\psi_{a},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\rangle,

where one has used

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-8T^{0})\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})d\mathfrak{v}=-4\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}^{2}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-8T^{0})\mu_{0}(\mathfrak{v})d\mathfrak{v}=0.

Similarly, one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|a_{\lambda}|^{2}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\,Cd|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+Cd^{2}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta

(4.48)

\displaystyle\quad+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2-2\mathfrak{a}}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2.4 Estimate on
b_{1,\lambda}
.
Multiplying (
4.34
) by
\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
, one has that

\displaystyle\rho^{0}T^{0}\partial_{\eta}b_{1,\lambda}+G(\eta)\rho^{0}T^{0}b_{1,\lambda}=-(1-\lambda)\langle\sqrt{\mu_{0}},\nu f_{\lambda}\rangle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v},

which yields that

\displaystyle\rho^{0}T^{0}\partial_{\eta}\left\{b_{1,\lambda}e^{-W(\eta)}\right\}=

\displaystyle-(1-\lambda)\langle\sqrt{\mu_{0}},\nu f_{\lambda}\rangle e^{-W(\eta)}

(4.49)

\displaystyle+e^{-W(\eta)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v}.

Due to the specular boundary condition at
\eta=0
, it holds that
b_{1,\lambda}(0)=0
. Then, integrating (
4.49
) over
[0,\eta]
, one gets that

\displaystyle e^{-W(\eta)}\rho^{0}T^{0}b_{1,\lambda}(\eta)=

\displaystyle-(1-\lambda)\int_{0}^{\eta}\langle\sqrt{\mu_{0}},\nu f_{\lambda}\rangle e^{-W(z)}dz

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\eta}e^{-W(z)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S(z)\cdot\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\,d\mathfrak{v}\,dz,

which implies that

\displaystyle|b_{1,\lambda}(\eta)|\leq C(1-\lambda)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Hence we have

(4.50)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|b_{1,\lambda}|^{2}d\eta\leq C(1-\lambda)^{2}d^{2}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2.5.
Noting
1-\lambda\leq\varepsilon
and
d=\varepsilon^{-\mathfrak{a}}
with
\mathfrak{a}<\frac{2}{3},\varepsilon\ll 1
, one has from (
4.42
), (
4.47
), (
4.48
) and (
4.50
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|(a_{\lambda},b_{\lambda},c_{\lambda})|^{2}\,d\eta\leq

\displaystyle\,Cd|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+Cd^{2}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{2-2\mathfrak{a}}\int_{0}^{d}|(a_{\lambda},b_{\lambda},c_{\lambda})|^{2}\,d\eta

(4.51)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,Cd|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+Cd^{2}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f_{\lambda}\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta+Cd^{2}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Noting
\lambda\geq 1-\varepsilon
, we have from (
4.36
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\left\|\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0}\right)f_{\lambda}\right\|_{\nu}^{2}\,d\eta+|f_{\lambda}(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}\left\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}^{2}+C\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\cdot\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

which, together with (
4.51
), yields that

(4.52)

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2-3\mathfrak{a}}\cdot\left\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}^{2}+Cd^{4}\|S\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{2}}^{2},\quad 1-\lambda\leq\varepsilon.

Step 3. Conclusion.
Combining (
4.52
) and (
4.37
), we conclude (
4.33
) holds. Therefore the proof of that Lemma
4.4
is completed.
∎

Then we aim to get the
\lambda
-independent
L^{\infty}
estimate. Define
K_{M,w}:=w(\mathfrak{v})K_{M}w(u)^{-1}.

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume
\beta>3,\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
. For any given
\lambda\in[0,1]
, let
f_{\lambda}
be the solution of (
4.11
). Let
\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f_{\lambda}
with
\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}<\infty
. Then it holds that

(4.53)

\displaystyle\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\lambda
and
d
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1.
The equation for
\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}
is

(4.54)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}-\lambda K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}=\bar{S},\\
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(0,\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}>0}=\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\quad\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\mathfrak{v}_{1}<0}=0.\end{cases}

Along the characteristics, (
4.54
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{d\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}}{ds}+\nu\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}-\lambda K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}=\bar{S}.

Let
h=w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}
, the equation of
h
is

\displaystyle\frac{dh}{ds}+\nu h-\lambda K_{M,w}h=w\bar{S},

which yields

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\left\{he^{\nu s}\right\}-\lambda K_{M,w}h\cdot e^{\nu s}=w\bar{S}\cdot e^{\nu s}.

For
Case 1.1
, we have

\displaystyle h(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}\lambda K_{M,w}h(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds

(4.55)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}w\bar{S}(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

For
Case 1.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle h(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}}\lambda K_{M,w}h(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds

(4.56)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}}w\bar{S}(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))\cdot e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

For
Case 2
, one has
E_{1}=V_{2}^{2}(\eta_{+}),0<\eta_{+}\leq d.
After
k
times collision with
\eta=0
, one has that

\displaystyle h(\eta,\mathfrak{v})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}}

\displaystyle=h(0,v_{1})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}_{1}}+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}(\lambda K_{M,w}h+w\bar{S})e^{\nu s}ds

\displaystyle=h(0,v_{2})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}_{2}}+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}}(\lambda K_{M,w}h+w\bar{S})e^{\nu s}ds

\displaystyle=...

\displaystyle=h(0,v_{k})e^{\nu\mathfrak{t}_{k}}+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}(\lambda K_{M,w}h+w\bar{S})e^{\nu s}ds,

which yields that

(4.57)

\displaystyle h(\eta,\mathfrak{v})=h(0,v_{k})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}}(\lambda K_{M,w}h+w\bar{S})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds.

To control the first term on the RHS of (
4.57
), we choose
k\sim\frac{1}{\eta_{+}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})}k_{0}
with
k_{0}\gg 1
, then one obtains

\displaystyle\displaystyle e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}\leq e^{-(k-1)\nu(\mathfrak{t}_{l}-\mathfrak{t}_{l+1})}\leq\exp\{-(k-1)\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l+1}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}|V_{1}|ds\}=e^{-2(k-1)\eta_{+}}\leq\frac{1}{8},

which, together with (
4.55
)–(
4.57
), yields that

(4.58)

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|h|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq\frac{1}{8}|h|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}+C\lambda\|K_{M,w}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Noting (
4.7
), one gets from (
4.58
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta-1,\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle...

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{0,\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

(4.59)

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where we have used the notations
\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}=\|(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}e^{\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}e^{\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}
and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}}^{2}=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|^{2}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})d\mathfrak{v}\right|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta}}.

Step 2.
By the mild formulation, one has

(4.60)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\lambda K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\,ds+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\bar{S}\,ds\quad\text{in \it Case 1.1, }

(4.61)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\lambda K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\,ds+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\bar{S}\,ds\quad\text{in \it Case 1.2, }

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(\eta,\mathfrak{v})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}(\eta_{k},v_{k})e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\lambda K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\,ds

(4.62)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\bar{S}ds,\quad\text{in \it Case 2}.

In
Case 2
, for the first term on the RHS of (
4.62
),
\eta_{k}
is actually
0
and we have to control

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|^{2}(0,v_{k})e^{-2\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}d\mathfrak{v}.
We know from (
4.26
) that

(4.63)

\displaystyle\eta_{+}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|^{2}/|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|^{2}}{\left(1+\sqrt{1+|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|^{2}/|\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}}\right)\sqrt{1+|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|^{2}/|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|^{2}}}+\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|}{|\mathfrak{v}|}\eta.

We observe that
\eta_{+}
increases with
\displaystyle|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|.
For
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m
, it holds that
\eta_{+}\geq\frac{m^{2}}{4\varepsilon^{2}}
. Then we choose
k=C(m)k_{0}
with
k_{0}\gg 1
such that

(4.64)

\displaystyle e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}\leq m.

Now we take
k=C(m)k_{0}
for
Case 2
in (
4.62
). If
|\mathfrak{v}|\geq\mathfrak{N}
, it is clear that

(4.65)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}|\geq\mathfrak{N}\}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|^{2}(0,v_{k})e^{-2\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}d\mathfrak{v}\leq\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}^{\beta}}\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

For
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m,|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N}
, one has from (
4.64
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m,|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N}\}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|^{2}(0,v_{k})e^{-2\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}d\mathfrak{v}\leq m\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

For
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\leq m,|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N}
, one has
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\leq m\mathfrak{N}
, the integral domain is small and

(4.66)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\leq m,|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N}\}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|^{2}(0,v_{k})e^{-2\nu(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}_{k})}d\mathfrak{v}\leq m\mathfrak{N}\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

Thus we obtain from (
4.60
)–(
4.66
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{m\mathfrak{N}}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}+\lambda\left\|\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|\,ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}}

(4.67)

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|\bar{S}|ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}},

where
k=1
for
Case 1.1,

k=2
for
Case 1.2
and
k=C(m)k_{0}
for
Case 2.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|\bar{S}|ds\right\|_{L^{2}_{\zeta}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\left[\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|\bar{S}|ds\right]^{2}d\mathfrak{v}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\left[\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}\nu w^{-1}ds\right]^{2}d\mathfrak{v}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{-\beta}d\mathfrak{v}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

which, together with (
4.67
), yields that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{\zeta}}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{m\mathfrak{N}}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

(4.68)

\displaystyle+C\lambda\left|\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\left\{\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|ds\right\}^{2}d\mathfrak{v}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta}}.

Step 3.
We now denote
\displaystyle\mathcal{J}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\zeta|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}}\left\{\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))ds\right\}^{2}d\mathfrak{v}
and split the integral into 4 parts.

Case i.

|\mathfrak{v}|\geq\mathfrak{N}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}=
