\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathfrak{v}|\geq\mathfrak{N}}e^{2\zeta|v|^{2}}\{\cdot\cdot\cdot\}^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}|\geq\mathfrak{N}\}}\frac{1}{(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{\beta}}\left\{\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{k}}^{\mathfrak{t}}e^{-\nu(\mathfrak{v})(\mathfrak{t}-s)}ds\right\}^{2}d\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\mathfrak{N}^{\beta}}\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{(1+|\mathfrak{v}|^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\nu^{2}(\mathfrak{v})}d\mathfrak{v}

(4.69)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\mathfrak{N}^{\beta}}\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\leq\frac{C}{\mathfrak{N}^{\beta}}\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

Case ii.

|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq m,|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m
. In this case, one has that

(4.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq m,,|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m\}}e^{2\zeta|v|^{2}}\{\cdot\cdot\cdot\}^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{N}e^{2\zeta\mathfrak{N}^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{t}_{l}}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq m,,|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\geq m\}}\frac{(K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda})^{2}}{\nu^{2}}(X_{cl}(s),V_{cl}(s))dsd\mathfrak{v}.

Let
z=X_{cl}(s)
, i.e.

\displaystyle z=\eta_{l-1}-\int_{s}^{\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}}V_{1}d\tau,\quad s\in(\mathfrak{t}_{l},\mathfrak{t}_{l-1}].

Noting
\eta_{k}=0
for all
k\geq 1
and
\eta_{0}=\eta
, one has that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{dz}{ds}\right|=|V_{1}|,

where

\displaystyle|V_{1}|=\sqrt{|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}-\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\left(e^{-2W(\eta)+2W(X(s))}\right)},\quad V_{2}=\mathfrak{v}_{2}e^{-W(\eta)+W(X(s))}.

Since
|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq m,|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},
noting the smallness of
\varepsilon
, we obtain

\displaystyle|V_{1}|^{2}\geq m^{2}+|\mathfrak{v}_{2}|^{2}\left\{1-\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}{1-\varepsilon^{2}X(s)}\right)^{2}\right\}\geq m^{2}-3\mathfrak{N}^{2}\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}\geq\frac{m^{2}}{4}.

Then we first take the change of variable
ds\to dz
and have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{ds}{dz}\right|\leq\frac{2}{m}.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial V_{cl}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|e^{-W(\eta)+W(z)}}{\sqrt{|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}-\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}e^{-2W(\eta)+2W(z)}}}=\frac{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|e^{-W(\eta)+W(z)}}{|V_{1}|}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-W(\eta)+W(z)}}{\sqrt{1+|\mathfrak{v}_{2}/\mathfrak{v}_{1}|^{2}\left\{1-\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon^{2}\eta}{1-\varepsilon^{2}z}\right)^{2}\right\}}}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+3\frac{\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}}{m^{2}}}}\geq\frac{1}{3},

which further yields that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial\mathfrak{v}}{\partial V}\right|=\frac{|V_{1}|}{|\mathfrak{v}_{1}|e^{-W(\eta)+W(z)}}\leq 3.

Then we take
d\mathfrak{v}\to dV
and obtain from (
4.70
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\frac{\mathfrak{N}e^{2\zeta\mathfrak{N}^{2}}}{m}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{d}\frac{(K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda})^{2}(z,V)}{\nu^{2}}dzdV\right\}

(4.71)

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\frac{\mathfrak{N}e^{2\zeta\mathfrak{N}^{2}}}{m}\|\nu^{-1}K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}.

Case iii.

|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},\ |\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\geq m,|\mathfrak{v}_{1}/\mathfrak{v}_{2}|\leq m
. In this case, the integral domain of
\mathfrak{v}_{1}
is small, then one gets

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}\leq Cm\mathfrak{N}\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\leq Cm\mathfrak{N}\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

Case iv.

|\mathfrak{v}|\leq\mathfrak{N},\ |\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\leq m
. In this case, the integral domain of
\mathfrak{v}_{1}
is also small, then one has

(4.72)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{4}\leq Cm\|K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\leq Cm\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

One gets from (
4.69
) and (
4.71
)–(
4.72
) that

(4.73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}\leq C_{m,\mathfrak{N}}\|\nu^{-1}K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C(m+m\mathfrak{N}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}^{\beta}})\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}^{2}.

Substituting (
4.73
) to (
4.68
) and (
4.59
), one has

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{m,\mathfrak{N}}\lambda\|\nu^{-1}K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{m\mathfrak{N}}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}}\right)\|\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\beta,\zeta}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

which, together with choosing
\mathfrak{N}\gg 1
and
m\ll\frac{1}{\mathfrak{N}}
, yields that

\displaystyle\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nu^{-1}K_{M}\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+C\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Therefor the proof of Lemma
4.5
is finished.
∎

Combining Lemmas
4.4
–
4.5
and noting
\varepsilon\ll 1,\mathfrak{a}<\frac{2}{3}
, we have the following result.

Corollary 4.1
.

Assume
\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty,\beta>3
. For any given
\lambda\in[0,1]
, let
f_{\lambda}
be the solution of (
4.11
) with
\displaystyle\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
. Let
\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f_{\lambda}
with
\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}<\infty
. It holds that

(4.74)

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{2}}+\left\|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}+|w\mathbf{f}_{\lambda}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C_{\varepsilon,d}\left\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\right\|_{L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}},

where
C_{\varepsilon,d}=\max\{\varepsilon^{-1}d,d^{3}\}
does not depend on
\lambda
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\beta>3
and
\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
. Then there is a unique solution
f
to equation (
4.4
) (or equivalently a unique solution
\mathbf{f}
to (
4.6
) with
\mathbf{f}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f
) satisfying

(4.75)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wf\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\left|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wf\right|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}<C_{\varepsilon,d}\|\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where the constant
C_{\varepsilon,d}
only depends on
\varepsilon
and
d
. Moreover, if
S
is continuous in
[0,d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, then
f
is continuous away from grazing set
\{(\eta,\mathfrak{v}):\eta=0,\mathfrak{v}_{1}=0\}
.

Proof.

Define Banach space:

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}:=

\displaystyle\left\{f=f(\eta,\mathfrak{v})|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wf\in L^{\infty}([0,d]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\text{and}\quad f(0,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=f(0,-\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2}),\,f(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0\right\}.

We denote a linear operator
T_{\lambda}
as

\displaystyle T_{\lambda}f:=\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf+S\}.

For any
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbf{X}
, we have from Lemma
4.3
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wT_{\lambda}(f_{1}-f_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\left[w\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf_{1}+S\}-w\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf_{2}+S\}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}w\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}\{K(f_{1}-f_{2})\}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\nu^{-1}wK(f_{1}-f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\lambda\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}w(f_{1}-f_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Take
\lambda_{*}
small such that
C\lambda_{*}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then
T_{\lambda}:\mathbf{X}\to\mathbf{X}
is a contraction mapping for
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{*}]
. Thus
T_{\lambda}
has a fixed point, i.e.,
\exists f_{\lambda}\in\mathbf{X}
, such that

\displaystyle f_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda}f_{\lambda}=\mathcal{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf_{\lambda}+S\},\quad\forall\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{*}],

which yields immediately

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}f_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}f_{\lambda}+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial f_{\lambda}}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)+\nu f_{\lambda}-\lambda Kf_{\lambda}=S.

Hence, for any
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{*}]
, we have solved (
4.11
) with
f_{\lambda}=\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1}S\in\mathbf{X}.
Moreover
f_{\lambda}
satisfies (
4.74
).

Noting the constant
C_{\varepsilon,d}
in (
4.74
) does not depend on
\lambda
. For any
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbf{X}
, we have from (
4.74
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}wT_{\lambda_{*}+\lambda}(f_{1}-f_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\left[w\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{*}}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf_{1}+S\}-w\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{*}}^{-1}\{\lambda Kf_{2}+S\}\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}w\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{*}}^{-1}\{K(f_{1}-f_{2})\}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon,d}\lambda\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\nu^{-1}wK(f_{1}-f_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon,d}\lambda\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}w(f_{1}-f_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

By similar arguments, we prove there exists a
\lambda_{1}>0
, such that
T_{\lambda_{*}+\lambda}
is a contraction mapping for
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{1}].
Then we obtain the existence of
operator
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{*}+\lambda}^{-1}
. Step by step, we can finally obtain the
existence of operator
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{-1},\,\forall\lambda\in[0,1]
. The estimate (
4.75
) follows directly from (
4.74
). Therefore the proof of Lemma
4.6
is completed.
∎

4.2.
Exponential spatial decay

We have obtained the solution of (
4.4
)(or equivalently (
4.6
)) from Lemma
4.6
and get some
L^{2},L^{\infty}
estimates relying on
d
. However, to construct the Hilbert expansion, spatial decay estimate is crucial.

Multiplying (
4.6
) by
e^{\sigma\eta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\partial_{\eta}\left(e^{\sigma\eta}\mathbf{f}\right)+G(\eta)\left(\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{2}}\right)\left(e^{\sigma\eta}\mathbf{f}\right)

(4.76)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{L}_{M}\left(e^{\sigma\eta}\mathbf{f}\right)=e^{\sigma\eta}\bar{S}+\sigma\mathfrak{v}_{1}\left(e^{\sigma\eta}\mathbf{f}\right).

Noting
\nu\sim 1+|\mathfrak{v}|
and
\sigma\ll 1
, applying Lemma
4.5
to (
4.76
), one obtains that

(4.77)

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq C\left\{\|e^{\sigma\eta}f\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\|e^{\sigma\eta}\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\right\}.

Thus we need to establish the uniform
L^{2}
estimate
\|e^{\sigma\eta}f\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}
.

Denote

(4.78)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{0}f=a\sqrt{\mu_{0}}+b_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}+b_{2}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}+c(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-2T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}.

Multiplying (
4.4
) by
\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
and integrating on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has from (
4.3
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}b_{1}+G(\eta)b_{1}=0,

which, with the specular property
b_{1}(0)=0
, yields that

(4.79)

\displaystyle b_{1}\equiv 0.

Similarly, multiplying
\mathfrak{v}_{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
and
|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
respectively with (
4.4
), using (
4.3
) and (
4.79
), one gets that

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\right\}+2G(\eta)\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\right\}=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\right\}+G(\eta)\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\right\}=0,

which yields that

(4.80)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|\mathfrak{v}|^{2}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}f\,d\mathfrak{v}\equiv 0.

Using (
4.79
)–(
4.80
) and noting (
4.3
), one deduces that

(4.81)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f|^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f\cdot S\,d\mathfrak{v}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\cdot S\,d\mathfrak{v}.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\beta>3,\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
for some
\sigma_{2}>0
and (
4.3
) hold. Let
f
be the solution of (
4.4
) constructed in Lemma
4.6
, it holds that

(4.82)

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma\eta}f\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+e^{\sigma d}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\sigma}}\varepsilon^{1-\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{2}}\|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\sigma(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}}\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where
\sigma\in(0,\sigma_{2})
and the constant
C>0
is independent of
d
and
\sigma
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1. Decay of microscopic part.
Multiplying (
4.4
) by
f\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}
, noting (
4.81
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\eta}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}\right\}-\frac{u^{0}_{\tau}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}+\langle f,\mathbf{L}_{0}f\rangle e^{-W(\eta)}

(4.83)

\displaystyle=\langle S,(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle e^{-W(\eta)}.

Multiplying (
4.83
) by
e^{2\sigma\eta}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\eta}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}\right\}-\frac{u^{0}_{\tau}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle+\langle f,\mathbf{L}_{0}f\rangle e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle S\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}+\sigma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}f^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}.

Then, integrating the above equation over
[0,d]
and using (
4.81
), we obtain

\displaystyle c_{0}\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}d\eta+\frac{1}{2}e^{2\sigma d}e^{-W(d)}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}\int_{0}^{d}G(\eta)e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f^{2}d\mathfrak{v}d\eta+\int_{0}^{d}\langle S,(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}d\eta

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma\int_{0}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathfrak{v}_{1}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f|^{2}\cdot e^{-W(\eta)}e^{2\sigma\eta}d\mathfrak{v}d\eta.

which, together with
\sigma\ll 1
and
e^{-W(\eta)}\sim 1
, yields

(4.84)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta+e^{2\sigma d}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2-\mathfrak{a}}\|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}+C\|e^{\sigma\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2. Decay of macroscopic part.

Step 2.1.
Taking
\varphi_{b}(\mathfrak{v})=\mathbf{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\}
, one has from (
4.80
) that

(4.85)

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f,\varphi_{b}\rangle=T^{0}\kappa_{1}(T^{0})b_{2}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\varphi_{b},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle,

and

(4.86)

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f,\varphi_{b}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f,\mathbf{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\}\rangle=\langle f,\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathfrak{v}_{2}-u_{\tau}^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\rangle=0,

where
\kappa_{1}
is the one defined in (
6.2
). Multiplying (
4.4
) by
\varphi_{b}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has from (
4.85
)–(
4.86
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}[T^{0}\kappa_{1}(T^{0})b_{2}+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\varphi_{b},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle]

\displaystyle=-G(\eta)\left\langle\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\varphi_{b}}{\partial v_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial(\mathfrak{v}_{2}\varphi_{b})}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}},f\right\rangle+\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f,\varphi_{b}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f,\varphi_{b}\rangle+\langle S,\varphi_{b}\rangle

(4.87)

\displaystyle=-G(\eta)\left\langle\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\varphi_{b}}{\partial v_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial(\mathfrak{v}_{2}\varphi_{b})}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}},f\right\rangle+\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(\eta)\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f,\varphi_{b}\rangle+\langle S,\varphi_{b}\rangle.

A direct calculation shows that

(4.88)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\eta}^{d}-G(z)\left\langle\mathfrak{v}_{2}^{2}\frac{\partial\varphi_{b}}{\partial v_{1}}-\mathfrak{v}_{1}\frac{\partial(\mathfrak{v}_{2}\varphi_{b})}{\partial\mathfrak{v}_{1}},f(z)\right\rangle\,dz\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz,\\
\int_{\eta}^{d}\frac{u_{\tau}^{0}}{2T^{0}}G(z)\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathfrak{v}_{2}f(z),\varphi_{b}\rangle\,dz\leq\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz.\end{split}

Integrating (
4.87
) on
[\eta,d]
, noting
f(d,\mathfrak{v})|_{\gamma_{-}}=0
, one obtains from (
4.88
) that

\displaystyle|b_{2}(\eta)|\leq

\displaystyle C|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}+C\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}

\displaystyle+C\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz+C\int_{\eta}^{d}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz,

which yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|b_{2}(\eta)|^{2}e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sigma}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma d}+C\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta

(4.89)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4-2\mathfrak{a}}\int_{0}^{d}\|f(\eta)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta+\frac{C}{\sigma_{2}(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2},

where we have used

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\left\{\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\right\}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{d}e^{2\sigma\eta}\int_{\eta}^{d}G^{2}(z)e^{-2\sigma z}dzd\eta\times\int_{0}^{d}\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma z}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{4-2\mathfrak{a}}\int_{0}^{d}\|f(\eta)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta,

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}\left\{\int_{\eta}^{d}\|S(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz\right\}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}d\eta\leq\left\{\int_{0}^{d}e^{2\sigma\eta}\int_{\eta}^{d}e^{-2\sigma_{2}z}dz\right\}\cdot\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma_{2}}\int_{0}^{d}e^{-2(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)\eta}d\eta\cdot\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sigma_{2}(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}\cdot\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2.2.
Taking
\varphi_{c}(\mathfrak{v})=\mathbf{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\}
, one has from (
4.80
) that

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f,\varphi_{c}\rangle=8(T^{0})^{2}\kappa_{2}(T^{0})c+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\varphi_{c},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f\rangle,

and

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f,\varphi_{c}\rangle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{L}_{0}f,\mathbf{L}_{0}^{-1}\{\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle f,\mathfrak{v}_{1}(|\mathfrak{v}-\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{0}|^{2}-4T^{0})\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\rangle=0,

where
\kappa_{2}
is the one defined in (
6.2
). Similar to (
4.89
), one gets that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|c(\eta)|^{2}e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sigma}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma d}+C\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta

(4.90)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4-2\mathfrak{a}}\int_{0}^{d}\|f(\eta)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta+\frac{C}{\sigma_{2}(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 2.3.
Taking
\varphi_{a}(\mathfrak{v})=\mathfrak{v}_{1}\sqrt{\mu_{0}}
, one has

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}f,\varphi_{a}\rangle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}\mathbf{P}_{0}f,\varphi_{a}\rangle+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f,\varphi_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{0}T^{0}a+2\rho^{0}(T^{0})^{2}c+\langle\mathfrak{v}_{1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f,\varphi_{a}\rangle.

Multiplying (
4.4
) by
\varphi_{a}
, we have that

\displaystyle|a(\eta)|\leq

\displaystyle 2|c(\eta)|+C|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}+C\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}

\displaystyle+C\int_{\eta}^{d}|G(z)|\cdot\|f(z)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz+C\int_{\eta}^{d}\|S\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\,dz,

which, together with (
4.90
), yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|a(\eta)|^{2}e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sigma}C|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma d}+C\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta

(4.91)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4-2\mathfrak{a}}\int_{0}^{d}\|f(\eta)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta+\frac{C}{\sigma_{2}(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2}.

Step 3. Conclusion.
It follows from (
4.89
), (
4.90
) and (
4.91
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}|(a,b_{2},c)|^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}d\eta\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sigma}e^{2\sigma d}|f(d)|_{L^{2}(\gamma_{+})}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{d}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})f(\eta)\|_{\nu}^{2}\cdot e^{2\sigma\eta}\,d\eta

(4.92)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\sigma_{2}(\sigma_{2}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}^{2},

where
0<\sigma<\sigma_{2}.
From (
4.79
), (
4.84
) and (
4.92
), we conclude (
4.82
). Therefore the proof of Lemma
4.7
is completed.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\beta>3,\|e^{\sigma_{0}\eta}\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty
for some
\sigma_{0}>0
and (
4.3
) hold. Let
\mathbf{f}
be the solution of (
4.6
) established in Lemma
4.6
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}\leq\frac{C}{\sigma(\sigma_{0}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{0}\eta}\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where
\sigma\in(0,\sigma_{0})
and the constant
C>0
is independent of
d
and
\sigma
.

Proof.

Combining (
4.77
) and (
4.82
), we have

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+|e^{\sigma\eta}w\mathbf{f}|_{L^{\infty}(\gamma_{+})}+\|e^{\sigma\eta}f\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}\leq\frac{C}{\sigma(\sigma_{0}-\sigma)}\|e^{\sigma_{0}\eta}\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}},

where we have chosen
\sigma_{2}=(\sigma+\sigma_{0})/2
and used

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma_{2}\eta}S\|_{L^{2}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}=\left\{\int_{0}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{2\sigma_{2}\eta}S^{2}\,d\mathfrak{v}d\eta\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\sigma_{0}-\sigma_{2}}}\|e^{\sigma_{0}\eta}\nu^{-1}w\bar{S}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}.

Therefore the proof of Lemma
4.8
is completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
:
Applying Lemma
4.8
to (
4.4
) which is in fact (
4.1
), noting (
4.2
), we now have proved Theorem
1.1
. The source term conditions (solvable conditions) (
4.3
) for (
4.1
) is actually (
1.37
) and
\mathfrak{S}\in\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\perp}
.
\hfill\Box

5.
Construction of Hilbert expansion and remainder estimate

5.1.
Construction of
F_{i},\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i},\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i},i=1,2,\cdots,N

Combining Section
2
to Section
4
, each layer can be constructed via a process similar as in
[
32
]
. Define the velocity weight functions

(5.1)

\displaystyle\varpi_{\kappa_{i}}(v)=\tilde{w}_{\kappa_{i}}(v)\mu^{\mathbf{-a}},\quad\bar{\varpi}_{\bar{\kappa}_{i}}(\bar{v})=\tilde{w}_{\bar{\kappa}_{i}}(v)\mu_{0}^{-\mathbf{a}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{i}}(\mathfrak{v})=\tilde{w}_{\hat{\kappa}_{i}}\mu_{M}^{-\mathbf{a}},

for constants
\kappa_{i},\bar{\kappa}_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{i}\geq 0,1\leq i\leq N
and
0\leq\mathbf{a}<\frac{1}{2}
. Denote
\bar{x}=(y,\phi),\hat{x}=(\eta,\phi)
and
\nabla_{\bar{x}}:=(\partial_{y},\partial_{\phi})
in viscous boundary coordinate.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
0\leq\mathbf{a}\textless\frac{1}{2}
in (
5.1
). Let
s_{0},s_{i},\bar{s}_{i},\hat{s}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\kappa_{i},\bar{\kappa}_{i},\ \hat{\kappa}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
1\leq i\leq N
; and define
l_{j}^{i}:=\bar{l}_{i}+2(\bar{s}_{i}-j)
for
1\leq i\leq N,0\leq j\leq\bar{s}_{i}
. For these parameters, we assume the restrictions

\displaystyle s_{0}\geq s_{1}+\mathfrak{b}+6,\quad s_{1}=\bar{s}_{1}=\hat{s}_{1}\gg 1;

(5.2)

\displaystyle s_{1}\textgreater s_{i}\textgreater\bar{s}_{i}\textgreater\hat{s}_{i}\geq s_{i+1}\textgreater\bar{s}_{i+1}\textgreater\hat{s}_{i+1}\geq...\gg 1,\ \text{for}\quad i=2,...,N-2;

\displaystyle s_{i+1}\leq\min\{\hat{s}_{i},\frac{1}{2}\bar{s}_{i}-j\},\bar{s}_{i+1}\leq s_{i+1}-8-\mathfrak{b},\hat{s}_{i+1}\leq\frac{1}{2}\bar{s}_{i+1}-2-\mathfrak{b},\ \text{for}\quad i=1,...,N-1;

\displaystyle l_{j}^{N}\gg 2\mathfrak{b}\quad and\quad l_{j}^{i}\geq 2l_{j}^{i+1}+18+2\mathfrak{b},\ \text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq N-1;

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}\gg\bar{\kappa}_{i}\gg\hat{\kappa}_{i}\gg\kappa_{i+1}\gg\bar{\kappa}_{i+1}\gg\hat{\kappa}_{i+1}\gg 1,

hold. Let the initial data
(\rho_{i},u_{i},\theta_{i})(0)
of IBVP (
2.3
), and initial data
(\bar{u}_{i}\cdot\vec{\tau},\bar{\theta}_{i})(0)
of IBVP (
2.7
) satisfy

(5.3)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\Big\{\sum\limits_{l+|\alpha|\leq s_{i}}\|\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}(\rho_{i},u_{i},\theta_{i})(0)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\bar{s}_{i}}\sum\limits_{2l+|\alpha|=j}\||\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}(\bar{u}_{i}\cdot\vec{\tau},\bar{\theta}_{i})(0)\|_{L_{l_{j}^{i}}^{2}}^{2}\Big\}\textless\infty.

We also assume the compatibility conditions for initial data
(\rho_{i},u_{i},\theta_{i})(0)
and
(\bar{u}_{i}\cdot\vec{\tau},\bar{\theta}_{i})(0)
are satisfied at the boundary. Then there exist solutions
\displaystyle F_{i}=\sqrt{\mu}f_{i},\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}=\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\bar{f}_{i},\hat{F}_{i}=\sqrt{\mu_{M}}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{i}
(or equivalently
\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{i})
to (
1.8
), (
1.22
), (
1.29
) over the time interval
t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]
, respectively. Moreover, we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{N}\Big(\sum\limits_{l+|\alpha|\leq s_{i}}\|\varpi_{\kappa_{i}}\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}f_{i}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v}}+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\bar{s}_{i}}\sum\limits_{j=2l+|\alpha|}\|\bar{\varpi}_{\bar{\kappa}_{i}}\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}\bar{f}_{i}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{l_{j}^{i}}L^{\infty}_{\bar{v}}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{l+|\alpha|\leq\hat{s}_{i}}\|e^{q_{i}\eta}\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{i}}\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{\phi}^{\alpha}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{i}(t)\|_{L_{\hat{x},v}^{\infty}\cap L_{\phi}^{2}L_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}^{\infty}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\tau,\|(\phi_{0},\Phi_{0},\vartheta_{0})\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\big\{\sum\limits_{l+|\alpha|\leq s_{i}}\|\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}(\rho_{i},u_{i},\theta_{i})(0)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

(5.4)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\bar{s}_{i}}\sum\limits_{2l+|\alpha|=j}\||\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{\bar{x}}^{\alpha}(\bar{u}_{i},\bar{\theta}_{i})(0)\|_{L_{l_{j}^{i}}^{2}}^{2}\big\}\Big).

where the positive constants
q_{i}\textgreater 0(i=1,...,N)
satisfying
q_{i+1}=\frac{1}{2}q_{i}
and
q_{1}=1
.

Proof.

Firstly, we aim to obtain the tangential and time derivatives estimates for
\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{k}
(or equivalently
\hat{f}_{k}
). We study the equation of
\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{\phi}^{j}(\sqrt{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}_{k}).
Using similar methods in Section
4
, one gets that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{i+j\leq\hat{s}_{k}}\|\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{k}}e^{q_{k}\eta}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{\phi}^{j}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{k}(t,\phi,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\|\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{k}}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{\phi}^{j}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{k}(t,\phi,0,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}<\infty.

Such an estimate (
5.5
) is enough for us to establish the higher order Hilbert expansion. Moreover, taking
L^{\infty}_{\phi}\cap L^{2}_{\phi}
over (
5.5
), one obtains

\displaystyle\sum_{i+j\leq\hat{s}_{k}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]}\Big\{\|\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{k}}e^{q_{k}\eta}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{\phi}^{j}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{k}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\phi}\cap L^{2}_{\phi}L^{\infty}_{\eta,\mathfrak{v}}}+\|\hat{\varpi}_{\hat{\kappa}_{k}}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial_{\phi}^{j}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{k}(t,\cdot,0,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\phi}\cap L^{2}_{\phi}L^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{v}}}\Big\}<\infty.

Secondly, we explain how (
2.25
) holds when constructing the Knudsen layer
\hat{f}_{k}=\hat{f}_{k,1}+\hat{f}_{k,2}.
Assume we have obtained
f_{k},\bar{f}_{k}
and
\hat{f}_{k,1}
. We can regularly obtain the velocity-weighted norm of
f_{k},\bar{f}_{k}
and
\hat{f}_{k,1}
and also the velocity derivative norm of
\mathbf{P}f_{k},\mathbf{P}_{0}\bar{f}_{k}
and
\hat{f}_{k,1}
. Noting (
1.15
) and (
1.25
), we also get the weighted velocity derivative norm of
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f_{k}
and
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\bar{f}_{k}
using the estimate of
\mathbf{L}^{-1}
in
[
42
]
. Then from the expression of
\hat{g}_{k}
in (
2.20
), we have (
2.25
) for each layer.

The rest proof of Proposition
5.1
is almost the same as
[
32
]
and we omit the details here. Then the proof of Proposition
5.1
is finished.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Recall Remark
1.2
, since the Knudsen layer
solutions
\hat{F}_{i}
are only defined in domain near the boundary of disk, it is necessary to extend
\hat{F}_{i}
into the whole disk by introducing
\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i}=\Upsilon(\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\eta)\hat{F}_{i}
, see also (
1.39
). Due to the exponential space decay on
\eta
and the cut-off function, it is direct to know that the error has a very high order
\varepsilon
-decay rate.

5.2.
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
estimate of remainder

For later use, we first recall
F_{i},\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i},\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i}
constructed in section
5.1
, and the equation (
1.41
) for remainder
F_{R}^{\varepsilon}
.

Define

(5.6)

\displaystyle f_{R}^{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}F^{\varepsilon}_{R}\quad\mbox{and}\quad h^{\varepsilon}_{R}:=\frac{\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}F_{R}^{\varepsilon}=\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}f_{R}^{\varepsilon},

then we have from (
1.41
) that

(5.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}_{R}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{R}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbf{L}f^{\varepsilon}_{R}=-\frac{\left\{\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\right\}\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\mu}}f^{\varepsilon}_{R}+S_{R},\\
\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,R_{x}v),\end{cases}

and

(5.8)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{t}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\tilde{\nu}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\tilde{K}_{M,\tilde{w}}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}=\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\bar{S}_{R},\\
\displaystyle h^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|_{\gamma_{-}}=h^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,R_{x}v).\end{cases}

Recall
\mathbf{L}
in (
1.12
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}g

\displaystyle=\tilde{\nu}(v)g-\tilde{K}g=\tilde{\nu}(v)g-(\tilde{K}_{1}g-\tilde{K}_{2}g)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\nu}(v)g(v)-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{1}(v,u)g(u)\,du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{2}(v,u)g(u)\,du\right),

where
\tilde{\nu}(v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|v-u|\mu(u)\,du\sim 1+|v|
and
\tilde{\nu}\geq\tilde{\nu}_{0}
with
\tilde{\nu}_{0}
a positive constant, and

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{1}(v,u)g(u)\,du=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|v-u|\sqrt{\mu(v)}\sqrt{\mu(u)}g(u)\,du

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{2}g

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{2}(v,u)g(u)\,du=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|v-u|\sqrt{\mu(u)}\sqrt{\mu(v^{\prime})}g(u^{\prime})\,du.

Denote
\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{M}g=\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\tilde{K}(\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}{\sqrt{\mu}}g)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{M}(v,u)g(u)\,du
and
\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{M,\tilde{w}}h=\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\tilde{K}(\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\frac{h}{\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}})
. Similar to Lemma
4.1
, one has

(5.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{k}_{M}(v,u)(1+|u|^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}e^{\zeta|u|^{2}}\,du\leq C(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{\beta-1}{2}}e^{\zeta|v|^{2}},\quad\beta\geq 0,\zeta<\frac{1}{4T}.

We divide
S_{R}
into three parts as
S_{R}=S_{R,1}+S_{R,2}+S_{R,3}
:

\displaystyle S_{R,1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{3}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon}_{R},f^{\varepsilon}_{R}),

(5.10)

\displaystyle S_{R,2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon^{i-2}\left\{\Gamma\left(f^{\varepsilon}_{R},\frac{F_{i}+\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}+\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right)+\Gamma\left(\frac{F_{i}+\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}+\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i}}{\sqrt{\mu}},f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle S_{R,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{R^{\varepsilon}+\bar{R}^{\varepsilon}+\hat{R}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\mu}},

where

\displaystyle\Gamma(g_{1},g_{2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q(\sqrt{\mu}g_{1},\sqrt{\mu}g_{2}).

Furthermore,
\bar{S}_{R}=\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\mu_{M}}}S_{R}
can be relatively divided into
\bar{S}_{R,1},\bar{S}_{R,2}
and
\bar{S}_{R,3}
.

We intend to establish the estimate of remainder
F_{R}^{\varepsilon}
by using the
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
framework.

5.2.1.
L^{2}
-estimate

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
0<\frac{1}{2\iota}(1-\iota)<\mathbf{a}<\frac{1}{2},\mathbf{k}\geq 6,N\geq 6
and
\mathfrak{b}\geq 5.
Let
\tau^{\delta}>0
be the life span of compressible Euler solution obtained in Lemma
2.1
. For
t\in[0,\tau^{\delta}]
, there exists a suitably small constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}^{2}\,dx

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{1+\varepsilon^{\min\{2\mathbf{k}-5,8\}}\|h^{\varepsilon}_{R}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}\right\}\cdot(\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+1).

Proof.

Multiplying (
5.7
) by
f^{\varepsilon}_{R}
and integrating on
B_{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, one has that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}^{2}\,dx

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|v|)^{3}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|^{2}dvdx+\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot S_{R}(x,v)|dvdx.

As in
[
32
]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|v|)^{3}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|^{2}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1}}\int_{|v|\leq\frac{\lambda}{\varepsilon}}(1+|v|)^{3}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|^{2}dvdx+\int_{B_{1}}\int_{|v|\geq\frac{\lambda}{\varepsilon}}(1+|v|)^{3}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}(x,v)|^{2}dvdx

(5.13)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}^{2}\,dx+C_{\lambda}(\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2\mathbf{k}-5}\|h^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}).

It is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot S_{R,1}|dvdx=\varepsilon^{3}\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon}_{R},f^{\varepsilon}_{R})|dvdx

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{3}\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon}_{R},f^{\varepsilon}_{R})|dvdx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}^{2}\,dx+C_{\lambda}\varepsilon^{8}\|h^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

Noting the discussion of
F_{i},\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}
and
\hat{F}_{i}
in Proposition
5.1
and recall (
1.39
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot S_{R,2}|dvdx

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon^{i-2}\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot\Gamma\left(f^{\varepsilon}_{R},\frac{F_{i}+\bar{\mathfrak{F}}_{i}+\hat{\mathscr{F}}_{i}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right)\right|\,dvdx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}\cdot\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\,dx\leq\frac{\lambda}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{B_{1}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{\tilde{\nu}}^{2}\,dx+C_{\lambda}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

where we have used (
1.35
) and obtained the following facts

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{\bar{\mathscr{F}_{i}}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\mu_{0}^{-\mathbf{a}}\bar{f}_{i}|\cdot\frac{\mu_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{a}}}{\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\mu_{0}^{-\mathbf{a}}\bar{f}_{i}|\cdot\mu_{M}^{(\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{a})\iota-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{\hat{F}_{i}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\mu_{M}^{-\mathbf{a}}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{i}|\cdot\frac{\mu_{M}^{\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{a}}}{\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq|\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\mu_{M}^{-\mathbf{a}}\hat{\mathbf{f}}_{i}|\cdot\mu_{M}^{\mathbf{a}}.

From Proposition
5.1
, noting (
1.42
) and (
1.43
), one deduces that
S_{R,3}\in L^{2}(B_{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and

(5.14)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\cdot S_{R,3}|dvdx\leq C(\varepsilon^{N-6}+\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{b}-5})\|f^{\varepsilon}_{R}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

Combining (
5.12
)–(
5.14
), taking
\lambda
small enough, one concludes Lemma
5.3
. Therefore the proof of Lemma
5.3
is completed.
∎

5.2.2.
L^{\infty}
-estimate

To study (
5.8
), we consider the change of variables. Let

x=(r\cos\phi,r\sin\phi),\quad\bar{\mathfrak{v}}=(\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})=(v\cdot\vec{n},v\cdot\vec{\tau})

where
\vec{n}=(\cos\phi,\sin\phi),\vec{\tau}=(-\sin\phi,\cos\phi)
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle v\cdot\nabla_{x}=\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\partial_{r}+\frac{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}}{r}(\partial_{\phi}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\partial_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\partial_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}}).

Now (
5.8
) is rewritten as

(5.15)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\partial_{r}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}+\frac{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}}{r}(\partial_{\phi}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\partial_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\partial_{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}})h^{\varepsilon}_{R}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\tilde{\nu}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\tilde{K}_{M,\tilde{w}}h^{\varepsilon}_{R}=\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{k}}\bar{S}_{R},

and the boundary is divided into three parts:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\gamma}_{-}=\{(r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}):r=1,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}<0\},\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{0}=\{(r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}):r=1,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}=0\},\\
\tilde{\gamma}_{+}=\{(r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}):r=1,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}>0\}.\end{split}

Definition 5.1
.

Define the backward characteristic for (
5.15
) as

(5.16)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{d\mathcal{X}}{ds}=\mathcal{V}_{1},\frac{d\Phi}{ds}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{2}}{\mathcal{X}},\frac{d\mathcal{V}_{1}}{ds}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{2}^{2}}{\mathcal{X}},\frac{d\mathcal{V}_{2}}{ds}=-\frac{\mathcal{V}_{1}\mathcal{V}_{2}}{\mathcal{X}},\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\displaystyle(\mathcal{\mathcal{X}},\Phi,\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2})(t)=(r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}).\end{cases}

Along the backward characteristic line, we have

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{X}=\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}r,\quad|\mathcal{V}_{1}|^{2}+|\mathcal{V}_{2}|^{2}=|\bar{\mathfrak{v}}|^{2}=|v|^{2},

which yields

(5.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2}(s)=\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\frac{r}{\mathcal{X}(s)},\quad|\mathcal{V}_{1}|=\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}.

For each
(t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})\notin\{\tilde{\gamma}_{0}\cup\tilde{\gamma}_{-}\}
and
|\bar{\mathfrak{v}}|\neq 0
, we define its backward time
t_{\mathbf{b}}(t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})\geq 0
as

\displaystyle t_{\mathbf{b}}(t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}):=\sup\left\{z\geq 0,\,r-\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{V}_{1}(s)ds<1,\ 0\leq t-\tau\leq z\right\}.

Let
(r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2})\in[0,1]\times[0,2\pi)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}\},\ (t_{0},r_{0},\phi_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})=(t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})
, and inductively define for
k\geq 0
that

(t_{k+1},r_{k+1})=(t_{k}-t_{\mathbf{b}}(r_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k}),1).

It follows from (
5.18
) that
|\mathcal{V}_{1}|
increases with
\mathcal{X}
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{k+1,1}=\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}r^{2}},\quad|\mathbf{v}_{k+1,2}|=|\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}|r.

Define the cycle as

(5.19)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{X}_{cl}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\mathbf{1}_{(t_{1},t]}\left\{r-\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}\,d\tau\right\}+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k+1},t_{k}]}(s)\left\{1-\int_{s}^{t_{k}}\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}d\tau\right\},\\
&\Phi_{cl}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\left(\phi-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{V}_{cl,2}}{\mathcal{X}_{cl}}(\tau)\,d\tau\right)\mod 2\pi,\\
&\mathcal{V}_{cl,2}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\frac{r}{\mathcal{X}_{cl}(s)}.\end{aligned}\right.

Next, we make some analysis on the behavior of above backward characteristic line.

Case a.
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\cdot r=0
.
Then one has
\mathcal{V}_{cl,2}\equiv 0
and
|\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}|\equiv|\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}|
. The backward characteristic line cycles between
\mathcal{X}=0
and
1
with constant speed
|\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}|
. If
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}>0
, the backward characteristic line moves to
\mathcal{X}=0
first and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\sum_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k,0},t_{k}]}(s)\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\sum_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k+1},t_{k,0}]}(s)(-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}).

If
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\leq 0
, the line comes to
\mathcal{X}=1
first and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\mathbf{1}_{(t_{1},t]}\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k,0},t_{k}]}(s)\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k+1},t_{k,0}]}(s)(-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}),

where we denote
t_{k,0}\in[t_{k+1},t_{k}]
to be the time of backward characteristic reaching
\mathcal{X}=0
. The reason
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}
changes its sign at this point is actually that the normal vector
n
turns to the opposite direction.

Case b.
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\cdot r\neq 0
.
Then there exists a
x_{+}\in(0,1]
such that
|\mathcal{V}_{1}|=0
at this position and the backward characteristic line will cycle in
x\in[x_{+},1]
. We have from (
5.18
) that

(5.20)

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{x_{+}^{2}}=1+\frac{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}^{2}}{\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}}.

For later use, we denote
t_{k,0}\in(t_{k+1},t_{k}]
to be the time so that
\mathcal{V}_{1}(t_{k,0})=0
or equivalently
\mathcal{X}=x_{+}
.

If
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}>0
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k,0},t_{k}]}(s)\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k+1},t_{k,0}]}(s)(-1)\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}.

If
\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}\leq 0
, one obtains

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{cl,1}(s;t,r,\phi,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})=\mathbf{1}_{(t_{1},t]}(-1)\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k,0},t_{k}]}(s)\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}+\sum\limits_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{1}_{(t_{k+1},t_{k,0}]}(s)(-1)\sqrt{|v|^{2}-\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\mathcal{X}^{2}(s)}}.

The backward characteristic in polar coordinates are indeed consistent with those in original coordinates. See the figures below for instance.

(x,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})
(x_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})
(x_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})

(a)

\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\cdot r=0
.

(x,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})
(x_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{+}
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x_{+}

(b)

\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{2}\cdot r\neq 0,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{1}>0
.

Figure 3.
Backward characteristics in unit disk.

\mathcal{X}=0
\mathcal{X}=1
(r,\bar{\mathfrak{v}})
(r_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})
(r_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k})
